## Benentine Boldoran

Reverend Father Benentine Boldoran was the leader of the Ilmatari monastic order known as the Ramedaran Brotherhood, circa the . He administered the Cloister of St. Ramedar, a combination prison and sanitarium in the Starspire Mountains of Tethyr.

Description
Father Benentine was a rotund, balding man, as of 1370 DR. He ate often and kept his bald pate polished. He wore simple clothes, having taken a vow of poverty.

Personality
The leader of the Ramedarans had a keen wit and a boisterous sense of humor, which he used to drive his staff with lighthearted cajolery and verbally fence with nobles and rulers that didn't agree with his methods or mission. He believed in the tenets put forth by Saint Ramedar—that the penal system should serve the public by rehabilitating criminals, reforming and educating them to reenter society, rather than be used as an instrument of retribution or a specter of deterrence.

Abilities
Despite his round belly, Father Benentine was an expert in many martial art styles.

Possessions
Having taken a vow of poverty, the Reverend Father had very few possessions of any value.

Activities
Father Benentine was in charge of about 115 other monks and painbearers that resided and worked in the Cloister, and together they guarded and cared for approximately 225 prisoners (both criminal and political) and mentally ill patients. The Cloister also housed a great library of holy and scholarly works pertaining to the Ilmatari faith, as well as meticulous records of the activities and treatments of all those entrusted to their care.

Relationships
Benentine's humble nature and good humor in dealing with his subordinates earned him the great respect and loyalty of this staff, even as he drove them to meet exacting his exacting standards.  He was devotedly doting to his pet tressym, Timoni, who had free run of the echoing hallways and chambers of the Cloister. Only Father Benentine knew that Timoni was actually a young bronze dragon named Cuprantimonitinam.

Appendix
References

## Belmer

Belmer of the Six Thunders was a local archwizard of Calimshan. As of, he was working as a "firestop" mage on a roadside inn, "The Vizier Vanquished". He was the last one to die in the Night of Fourteen Magisters.

Personality
Belmer was competent but cruel.

Relationships
Though Turnock Draether had to pretty much trick him to be accepted as an apprentice, the young man soon came to hate him. Belmer, for his part, did not seem to trust him.

Abilities
Belmer's "six thunders" were battle spells. He knew a spell that created a magical tattoo, through which he could deal out an agonizing death to the person so tattooed.

History
Belmer took on Turnock Draether as an apprentice after the latter helped him home in Zazesspur during a night in which he got too drunk to even walk on his own, though this was also because Belmer was rising in power and influence, and so needed an apprentice to carry out some of the tasks. However, he put a tattoo on Turnock that would kill him painfully if he ever betrayed him.

During the Night of Fourteen Magisters, Belmer had a commission as the "firestop" mage of the Vizier Vanquished inn in northern Calimshan. After waking up, he quickly slew Illinge Hardraw with a spell from afar, while looking into a crystal ball, and soon gained the office of the Magister. After this act, he was quickly slain by his apprentice, Turnock Draether, who split his skull with an axe.

## Belnimbra's Street

Belnimbra's Street was a road located in the city of Waterdeep.

Location
Belnimbra's Street was found in southern Waterdeep within the Dock Ward and Castle Ward. The street originated in the Castle Ward, branching south from Soothsayer's Way near Snail Street. Belnimbra's Street wound south, passing into the Dock Ward where it was intersected by Lackpurse Lane. The street continued southwest toward the waterfront, terminating at Sail Street near the southern end of Lackpurse Lane. Other streets intersecting Belnimbra's Street included Rainrun Street, Ward's Way, Sakiir's Street, Eel Street, Net Street, Redcloak Lane, Wastrel Alley, Gut Alley, and the Slide.

## Beef tea

Beef tea was a savory drink often used as "cure" for a hangover and general cold sickness.

Description
The so-called beef tea was concocted by making meat broth, mixing it with hot kitchen drippings from roasts, a dash of brandy, sherry, or even zzar. The name remained the same no matter what kind of meat was used to make stock or what roasts were going in the kitchens, and the alcohol used often was the dregs.

In Cormyr and the Dalelands, venison was the most common meat used in mixing a swirl of beef tea, while such meats as old oxen, boars, moose, cattle, hogs were less common. Wild animal meats, such as bears and raccoons, were also not unheard of.

Appendix
References

## Beeswax

Beeswax was a chunk of wax made from melting beehives of either bees or giant bees.

Description
This substance typically had a dark yellow coloration.

Availability
 Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold beeswax for 1 silver piece per . The catalogue also sold scented beeswax and candles made of the substance.
 In the city of Skullport, the business Sisters Three Waxworks sold small and slender scented candles made of beeswax.

Usages
 Beeswax was commonly used to make candles, which were often used in the practice of clerical candle magic.
 Beeswax was occasionally used by craftsmen in the Sword Coast for a variety of leatherwork and woodwork.
 An ointment for reducing tumors could be made by mixing beeswax with powdered jet.
 Beeswax  could double as sealing wax.
 Some sets of thieves' tools included a lump of beeswax in order to make impressions of keys.
 Beeswax acted as a material component for a variety of spells, including deafness, inferno, and tears of fire.
 Alahandra's questing call, the Zakharan spell fire truth, as well as its more powerful form flame of justice, all required a candle made from beeswax as a material component. However, the latter two spells specifically required candles made from the beeswax of a rare species of desert bee.

## Beb-Shuur

Beb-Shuur, along with his esteemed colleague Xianga, was a member of the short-lived Order of Theorosophers, active in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Beb-Shuur's clothes and body language betrayed his completely self-absorbed nature. He spent his days engrossed in intellectual narcissism. He was a fawning university reject.

History
Beb-Shuur and Xianga both followed the teachings of the doctrine of Theorosophy formulated by Master Vugu of Cormyr. Unlike Philosophy, Theorosophy was not based on logic and empiricism, instead relied on deductive reasoning and intuition. In the, both Master Theorosophers, Beb-Shuur, and Xianga, pondered the world and their role in the Order of Theorosophers atop the Blade Cliffs, to the southwest of the town of Daggerford. Beb-Shuur exercised his impressive Theorosopher skills observing the siege camp filled with the knight of Sir Griswald. He deduced that it was a traveling circus because the camp had brightly-colored tents, good-looking men, and women performing drills. That was Beb-Shuur's initial assumption, and the doctrine of Theorosophy dictated he reinforced the assumption via deductive reasoning, believing that intuition was the key to understanding the true nature of the multiverse.

Beb-Shuur and Xianga argued over who would become the Master Theorosopher of the Order of Theorosophers following master Vugu's demise. His death came when he lept off a cliff believing that his physical body served as an anchor that kept the human spirit from flying. Vugu thought that the experiment served to prove that intuition was superior to empiricism, and as the next Master Theorosopher, both Beb-Shuur and Xianga wanted to follow their master's suit.

In reality, Vugu of Cormyr committed suicide, being swallowed by despair, seeing what idiocy his doctrine of Theorosophy was reduced to. His true thoughts and feelings were written in a journal washed ashore not far where his body plummeted atop the jagged rocks. When shown the journal, Beb-Shuur denied its authenticity based on a gut feeling as dictated by Theorosophy. Subsequently, both sages convinced each other that the only way to decide who was worthy of the title and leadership role was to follow Vugu's example. Both dimwitted men died after jumping off a cliff.

## Belvrost

The Belvrost clan was a magically powerful family of Reddansyr on the Dragon Coast known in the late 14 century DR

Description
Belvrosts were mostly burly, with brown hair and eyes on faces described as bearlike and friendly. However, some female members of their blood instead grew slender, with green eyes and red hair. Almost all of them had the spark for the Art.

History
The clan was based out of Reddansyr, where its members had largely numbered root-crop farmers and masons. They also owned some of the local inns. During his reign as the Magister from  to, Maxiladanarr Torstren arranged for marriages in this clan that would produce more mages.

By, the best known Belvrosts were Chasler Belvrost and Telgerd Belvrost.

Structure
The clan had outposts in Baernwood, Redwater, and Nathlekh. They numbered maybe two hundred mages of some skill, twenty of them being quite worthy of the title of a wizard. The patriarch and matriarch, Maelaeve Belvrost and Arildan Belvrost, lived in the manor of Sundstone in Baernwood by .

Powers
One or two Belvrosts every generation could "read items", perceiving mental scenes of the past events or owners of those items by touch, and the ability to send and receive mental missives in a manner akin to sending. All Belvrosts had the ability to respond to such a sending.

Rumors & Legends
Belvrosts were largely considered trustworthy, if a bit strange. They themselves believed Mystra had created them for a purpose yet unknown, and liked to pass down legends of their magically powerful members, such as Narvar Belvrost and Halbadan Belvrost.

## Belvyre

Belvyre was a noble-born scholar and Circle of the Land druid who lived in the Ten Towns of Icewind Dale during the late 15 century DR.

Description
He was rather small and seemed child-like in may respects, especially when in the presence of his bodyguard companion.

Personality
The halfling scientist was honest and unassuming when speaking to others. He poured his heart into his work, with the sole goal of making the Ten Towns a better place for all of its residents. He was however extremely naïve, overly trusting of strangers, and quite susceptible to flattery.

Abilities

Belvyre was an avid herbalist and botanist, and spent all his time studying myriad variety of plant life. He had a magnificent underground greenhouse where he could safely cultivate his plants in an environment safe from the frigid climate of the Frozenfar.

He had the adorable ability of transforming into a house cat.

Possessions

Among his most cherished possessions was his walking stick, a family heirloom that he always carried during his journeys across Icewind Dale. It was in fact a sheathed frostbrand sword, that possessed the power to absorb flame and intense heat, and shoot blasts of cold energy.

Activities

Among his goals were to locate sprigs of sacred winter plants that could be used to help feed the people of the Ten Towns.

Relationships
Belvyre's dearest friend was his long-time bodyguard Patience, a fearsome tiefling warrior who had watched over him since he was a child. He gave her endearing nicknames like "Patty Cake" or "Patootie".

He was also friends with the dragonborn ranger Saarvin, who helped with his experiments by gathering specimens out in the wilderness, and was a contemporary of the wizard Zeddicus.

History

When the Everlasting Rime struck Icewind Dale in the, Belvyre was hired by the council of the Ten Towns to help preserve and regrow the dying plants within their food stores. While Belvyre was working on a solution for the dying plant life, he was introduced by his friend Saarvin to two other companions investigating the Everlasting Rime, the barbarian named Runa and a mysterious warlock called Amos. During their first meeting, Amos suggested they journey to an underground cavern that led to a magical city that was buried deep beneath the Reghed Glacier.

The group set out across Icewind Dale for the glacier, and were eventually forced to take refuge in a cave to keep safe from the elements. Just after settling in they were attacked by frost giant skeletons and were forced to defend themselves. During the battle, Amos turned on the group by abducting Belvyre, stealing his frostbrand, and blasting the others off a cliff. He took Belvyre to an underground cavern full of sacred winter plants, which were nourished by the magic of a primordial shard. After sharing how he planned to exploit Belvyre's talents, Amos took him to the master of the primordial shard army.

As Belvyre's friends delved into the caverns Amos had taken Belvyre, Amos' master decided to use the primordial shard to trigger an underground volcanic eruption to destroy them. As his companions arrive to save him, Belvyre confronted Amos and urged him help save the duergar that would be inadvertently killed by the imminent disaster. He urged his freinds to spare Amos' life and protected him from their vengeance. As lava burst forth from the depths of the cavern, and the ground shook beneath them, Amos gave the frostbrand to Belvyre so he could save himself, their companions, and the unwary duergar. Belvyre managed to save Patience's life for once. Working together, the companions escaped to safety and stood poised ready to uncover the secrets of the long lost magical city.

## Bevis

Bevis the Illuminator was a scribe and illustrator that lived in Zhentil Keep during the 14 century DR.

Personality
While Bevis was quite skilled at illustrating tomes, he absolutely hated his job. The annoyances of ink stains that covered his hands, the acrid smell of paint and inevitable wrist cramps made his chosen profession insufferable.

Activities
He worked as an illuminator for over 15 years, adding artistic depictions to what were otherwise dry and boring texts. He much preferred working on 'penitentials', religious works that depicted scenes of the afterlife to those that were penitent. They gave him opportunity to paint vivid depictions of how he imagined the denizens of the City of Strife in Hades.

Relationships
Bevis was the father of Rinda, an especially skilled scribe of Zhentil Keep.

History
Some time during or before the, Bevis was hired by the church of Cyric to illustrate a tome that depicted scenes from the Time of Troubles, the year when the gods of Faerûn walked as mortals on Toril. Some time just prior to receiving the book, Bevis had recommended to the church his daughter Rinda work on subsequent drafts.

The book Bevis received was one of the early drafts of the Cyrinishad. Bevis read the entire book, unable to put it down once he began. Afterwards he was by Xeno Mirrormane and Fzoul Chembryl, high-ranking lords of Zhentil Keep. Unfortunately for him, Bevis was not affected by the intended magic within the book and did not believe its contents as irrefutable truth. For that, he was slain by Cyric's own avatar upon the blade of Godsbane.

After his death, Bevis' soul languished as one of the Burning Men that hung from within Cyric's palace in the City of Strife.

## Beast master

A beast master, also spelled beastmaster, was a ranger that formed a remarkable and profound bond with a certain natural beast..

Activities
Rangers that began the path of the beast master attracted a bestial companion that accompanied them on their journeys. It learned to follow simple commands, fighting alongside one another with exceptional coordination. Beast masters tended to believe their companions were more reliable and often capable than other people.

Culture
Beast masters were solitary individuals that were more at home in the wilderness, surrounded by the wild animals they so cherished. Interestingly enough, they often made their livings as hunters and fishers. Civilized folks often did not take kindly to beast masters and did not find them as charming as other outdoorsmen might. In turn, some more maligned beast masters became frightened of and even hateful towards city-dwelling folks of the Realms.

In the 14 century DR, beast masters could be found in Faerûn across the Sword Coast, all the way up to the Icewind Dale region, as well as in west Faerûn in the nations of Amn and Tethyr.

Abilities
Over time, the bond between a beast master and their animal companion was so strong, the ranger could experience the animal's visual perception by means of their telepathic link. Despite its obvious benefits, the bond could also be harmful to the beast master. Whenever their companion was struck in combat or otherwise harmed, the psyche of the ranger was damaged in kind.

Beast masters possessed a form of limited telepathy with all of animal kind, up to a range of about . Some could even communicate verbally with animals of the wild. As they became more experienced, some beast masters could even call upon hordes of wild animals to follow basic commands.

Whenever the most experienced beast masters cast primal spells centered upon themself, their companion beast would also receive its benefits.

Notable Beast Masters
 Z'neth, the hobgoblin zookeeper of the Wandering Emporium.

## Bellorath

Bellorath was a minotaur cult leader who dwelt within the Heartcoil Deeps of the Northdark as of the late 15 century DR. He had previously been human.

Personality
Bellorath was known for his rage and anguish. He personally attacked any travelers who entered his territory, and was not too proud to retreat in order to draw combatants into a trap.

Activities
Bellorath led a cult of minotaurs, and ruled over a small but labyrinthine domain in the Heartcoil Deeps. In order to sustain and grow his following, the cult preyed on travelers and explorers in the surrounding Underdark.

History
As a human, Bellorath once led a failed uprising against Chauntea's Citadel, after which he and his followers fled into the Heartcoil Deeps and were never heard from again. This did not mean he was idle or dead, however, as he spent his exile seeking out fiendish power in order to punish his enemies. The Deeps were known for access to such powers, and soon his efforts transformed him and his cultists into minotaurs.While not stated in Warriors of Waterdeep, given that Bellorath contacted a fiendish power that turned him into a minotaur, it may be assumed that his patron was Baphomet. This allowed them to carve out a domain within the Deeps, and over the years, they grew their cult by attacking travelers and striking out at their enemies one by one. Their expansion disrupted the hunting grounds of many dangerous creatures, notably umber hulks.

In the meantime, Bellorath had became something of a legend among some folk from Harvestshield Mountain. Eventually, two minor nobles—Elcharn and Cielaro—went into the notoriously dangerous Heartcoil Deeps to learn what had happened to him. Upon encountering them, Bellorath attacked and separated Elcharn and Cielaro, leading to their tragic demises elsewhere in the Heartcoil Deeps.

In the late 15 century DR, Bellorath and his cult clashed with a neighboring clan of stone giants who were fleeing their home following an incursion by mind flayers led by Qholillaroon. Not long after, a group of adventurers from Waterdeep arrived to investigate what the giants were fleeing from. Bellorath rushed out to attack them, but was driven off in the fighting.

## Belt of Raumathar

Belt of Raumathar was a powerful enchanted item uncovered in the Ring of Gray Flames of the Endless Wastes sometime before the late 14 century DR.

Powers
This belt fortified the wearer's abilities to wield arcane magics by giving them superior focus in handling evocation spells and granting the skill to weave penetrating spells. Additionally, the belt of Raumathar allowed the wearer to cast several powerful versions of the spells fireburst, magic missile, prismatic spray, and sunburst once per day, while the magic missile spell could be slung thrice per day.

History
It was unknown if the belt of Raumathar was a unique item, but one such waistband was recovered from the ruins known as the Ring of Gray Flames in the Endless Wastes sometime before the . The name of Raumathar being given to the belt was scholarly speculation on its unknown origins; however, magics weaved into the belt suggested it was in use by Raumathari evokers. As the belt of Raumathar granted the wearer abilities that a Raumathari battlemage already could harness with ease, sages speculated that it belonged to an ally or a friend of a Raumathari battlemage.

In 1374 DR, the belt of Raumathar was worn by a Red Wizard of Thay, Khai Khmun, during his confrontation with the Kalach-Cha and Safiya in the Plane of Shadow echo of Mulsantir.

## Belib's everlasting torch

Belib's everlasting torch was a unique mace that was initially designed to be an inextinguishable lantern.

Description
Belib's everlasting torch was a lantern, tastefully designed and lit with bright orange flames.

Powers
Belib's magic weapon possessed only one enchantment, the mace's head was constantly lit aflame and could not be extinguished by any means. It kept burning in water, buried underground, and when swung at beasts. When used in battle, it burned the target of the weapon's blows and had a great chance of lighting them on fire, dealing even more devastating fire damage.

History
The everlasting torch once belonged to Belib the Wary of Waterdeep, sometime before the . Belib was a gnome who believed himself to be the most well-equipped adventurer in the Realms, despite the fact that he'd never left his home to participate in the said adventures. Belib's everlasting torch was one among many powerful items enchanted to be used by adventurers in their dangerous trade. He spent years creating his equipment, and one day, he finished his last item, packed them in a sizable rucksack, and stepped out of his home, ready for a grand escapade. His adventure ended before it began as the gnome tripped over his stoop and was crushed by his own equipment. The bag split open, scattering all of his precious gear, immediately claimed by the endless Waterdhavian passersby.

Eventually, Belib's body was raised by a priestess, but he returned to his home, just like his rucksack – completely looted. Disheartened, the gnome created a handful of magic items for the priestess, who returned him to life and left Waterdeep for his homeland. As of the, Belib's everlasting torch was in the hands of a conniving drow merchant, Nym, who traveled the North and sold his magical curios. The drow was still in possession of the mace during his visit to the Wandering Village, east of the town of Kuldahar in 1312 DR.

## Bellows root

Bellows root was a common forest plant that possessed emetic properties used in medicine and was found in the Wood of Sharp Teeth in the Western Heartlands.

Description
Bellows root could be crushed and drunk in a tea in order to provoke vomiting in the imbiber, human and dragon alike. Bellows root looked similar to another plant, maiden's milk, which possessed antitussive properties.

History
Long before the, bellows root became a part of history when the logging camp of Hornhollow at the edge of the Wood of Sharp Teeth was visited by a senile red dragon, Craum Straug, whose dire cough lit the settlement ablaze. A traveling minstrel, Aznee Fraystrings, was tasked with removing the dragon and thought that he could prepare maiden's milk tea to help the wyrm like it helped his elderly uncle once. Aznee, however, made a mistake and made a bellows root tea instead, causing the dragon to vomit the tea out along with his draconic flame breath, incinerating the minstrel instantly. This vomiting fit eventually killed the geriatric dragon.

Bear warriors tapped into the spirit of a bear, gaining immense strength and ferocity during combat. They could transform into a bear when enraged. ## Ben-hadar

Ben-hadar, called the Prince of Good Water Creatures, the Squallbringer, and the Valorous Tempest, was the good archomental of the Elemental Plane of Water, and thus the counterpart and enemy of Olhydra. His symbol was a blue-green wave in a golden circle.

Description
He appeared as a rush of pure blue-green water, or as a masculine humanoid formed of that same water, some  tall (or long) and weighing approximately . Where his eyes were, the water sparkled like the sun shining on waves, and instead of hands he had a pair of crustacean-like claws.

Personality
Ben-hadar was a gruff warrior who seemed loud, boisterous, arrogant, and aggressive, but on the inside he was serene as a still pool. He was a deeply emotional and intuitive person, and one who would sacrifice himself to save his plane and his followers, whom he deeply cared for and nurtured as needed. The biggest flaw in his personality was his unwillingness to look beyond the Plane of Water. He was easily disappointed, bored, and dejected; and just as easily cheered up by those he cared for.

His experiences during the great war between Law and Chaos taught him to trust no one but himself, and that evil could come from all sides. He believed that seeking out evil to destroy was a fool's errand and that even evil creatures could be used for good purposes.

Abilities
Like all archomentals, Ben-hadar could shapechange into any creature of his elemental subtype at will, could innately command any water elementals within  of him, and could use the spell-like abilities detect good, detect evil, greater dispel magic, greater teleport, see invisibility, suggestion, and telekinesis at will. He could speak to and understand any creatures as if under the effects of the tongues spell. His more elemental spell-like abilities included being able to replicate the effects of bless water, control water, create water, and obscuring mist at will;fog cloud, solid fog, wall of water (similar to a triple-strength wall of ice, and only underwater), and quickened control water thrice per day; and horrid wilting, which he used only on truly evil creatures, once each day.

Also like other archomentals, he was immune to effects that would paralyze, petrify, stun, poison, or put him to sleep; healed quickly; and was highly resistant to both spells and physical damage. He couldn't even be harmed at all by weapons without at least a moderately powerful enchantment, and his watery form meant that slashing weapons were particularly ineffective. His mastery of water meant that creatures attacking him while in water were at a distinct disadvantage.

Ben-hadar could turn his form into an  vortex of water at will, a massive whirlpool that posed a serious hazard to any creature that was caught in it. He could also affect the movement of boats and ships at whim, capsizing ships up to  long and holding motionless crafts as much as  long. Ben-hadar radiated a  aura of dampness that extinguished any open, nonmagical flames; he could extinguish magical flames with but a touch.

Once per day, Ben-hadar could summon up to five elder tojanidas, three elder water elementals, eight nereids, one water monolith, or one hundred tritons to serve and aid him.

Possessions
Realm
His realm was the Coral Reef of Ssesurgass, and his command post there was a massive fortress hidden in the reef and guarded by colossal coral golems. A natural portal in his realm, guarded by a great sisiutl named Triusis, connected it to the Court of Stars.

Activities
The odd peace Ben-hadar had with Olhydra meant that he spent most of his time watching over and promoting the general welfare of those who lived in his realm. He still battled evil when necessary, but warred with no one who did not give him a very good reason to do so. Overall, he kept his affairs and doings private.

Relationships
Enemies
Ben-hadar, unlike the other good archomentals, had no great rivalry with his evil counterpart Olhydra; rather, the two seemed to have a tentative truce and any conflict between their servants was only incidental. Instead, the ooze archomental Bwimb (and later, Bwimb II) was the biggest thorn in the Squallbringer's side, frequently polluting the pure waters that Ben-hadar nurtured. He also actively opposed the minions of Sekolah and Panzuriel.

Ben-hadar kept the marid of the Citadel of Ten Thousand Pearls in line. He had a great rivalry with Chan, the good archomental of Air, and Zaaman Rul, the good archomental of Fire, because of his unwillingness to look beyond the concerns of the Plane of Water; with Chan, at least, the feud meant that he would not help her or anyone allied or serving her.

Allies
Ben-hadar had a truce with Charon: in exchange for that archfiend keeping fiendish troublemakers from using vortexes between the River Styx and the Plane of Water, Ben-hadar restrained his followers from interfering with Charon's own business. Another unusual ally and friend was the slaad lord Rennbuu, who sometimes visited Ssesurgass to admire and transform the colorful coral reefs; Ben-hadar appreciated his art and would provide him with materials and inspiration.

The Valorous Tempest was on good terms with the deities Deep Sashelas, Eadro, and Persana, and he was known to aid the mysterious Water Lion as well.

Servitors
Ben-hadar was directly served by water elementals, water mephits, and water weirds, who helped him run his dominion. Tritons, aquatic elves, ocean giants, and darfellan also inhabited his realm.

History
Like the other three Princes of Elemental Good, Ben-hadar was said to have been birthed by the Elemental Planes to balance out the evil of his counterpart Prince of Elemental Evil, Olhydra. With his four compatriots, he initially joined the side of Law and the Wind Dukes of Aaqa during the great war between Law and Chaos in the Age Before Ages. However, after Ehkahk and Chilimba, two lesser-known evil archomentals who also fought for Law, betrayed the four Princes of Elemental Good and then ambushed and killed the then-good archomental of Fire, Bristia Pel, Ben-hadar joined the side of Chaos until the war ended.

Rumors & Legends
Among the celestial eladrin, there were rumors that Ben-hadar and their Queen Morwel were occasional lovers after they met during the great war.

## Becloaked

The Becloaked were a group of eerie and enigmatic druids who inhabited The Archdruid's Grove in Mosstone.

Organization
Druids only known as the Becloaked were truly a mysterious bunch. These individuals of unknown gender wore gray-colored thick, heavily-cowled robes that completely obfuscated their features, faces, and showed no skin.

Base of Operations
Three of these Becloaked druids were always seen patrolling the Archdruid's Grove under the commands of the Archdruid himself. The Becloaked slowly walked their strange routes, and no one seemed to know the patrols' purpose. On occasions, they gathered around a random tree and stayed motionless for several minutes until resuming the route. The Becloaked spoke rarely and always in harshly whispered questions when addressed, like "Don't you have anything better to do?" Young acolyte druids tended to avoid the Becloaked, moving to the opposite side of a path and scattering out of the robed figures' way. On the other hand, the watchghosts that inhabited the Archdruid's Grove paid the Becloaked no mind.

History
According to Volothamp Geddarm's testimony recorded in the infamous writer's guide to Amn and Tethyr published in the late 14 century DR, he once witnessed a glimpse of a Becloaked's hand – covered in tiny green iridescent scales. However, Volo was unsure if what he saw was simply a lightly-fitted glove.

## Bec de corbin

Bec de corbins were a type of polearm in the Realms that had an infamous reputation.

At a glance, this weapon bore a resemblance to a lucerne hammer. Bec de corbins had  poles, with an awl spike that was shorter and broader than that of a lucerne hammer, and a hooked blade that was the weapon's namesake and comparable to a heavy military pick's head. The other end of the weapon was backed by either a clawed or pronged hammer head like the lucerne hammer, a flat hammer head, or even an axehead.

These weapons were highly specialized for piercing or slicing through heavy plate armor with their crow-beak, and then using the weapon's other end to strike the opponent.

## Belarbrith

Belarbrith was a more exotic species of tough and hardy root vegetable that survived cold winters and closely resembled a carrot.

Description
Belarbrith's taste could be described as that of an apple pie (the variety that did not include cinnamon). The edible root was chestnut-brown, blotched with gray. It was chewy and hard like an overgrown carrot. The vegetable's flesh was lighter brown and flecked with dark red knots or nodes.

Availability
The wild vegetable could be foraged across the Eastern and Western Heartlands. However, by the late 15 century, it was over-consumed and hard to find near settlements or busy roads. Belarbrith was very commonly cultivated in the Dalelands, Cormyr, northern Sembia, and was gaining popularity on the Sword Coast North. In the northern lands of the Savage Frontier, near Everlund and Silverymoon, Belarbrith's growth rate was greatly reduced because of the weather, producing a small, thin vegetable, while southern regions birthed fat and plump roots size of a human forearm.

Usages
Belarbrith roots were thoroughly cleaned of soil and fine hair-like roots, eaten raw, or peeled and sliced into disks. The vegetable was also often used as a culinary thickener or for flavor contrast in pork stews. When boiled and mashed, belarbrith was cooked into a type of dessert soup often consumed in the winter.

## Beneath the surface

Beneath the surface was a spell of the alteration school of magic that allowed the caster to peek inside of an object or through a barrier.

Effects
When cast, the beneath the surface spell rendered the surface touched by the caster – transparent. This invisibility was only perceived by the caster. The spell's effect was not limited by the target's size. It equally affected small chests or castle doors, as long as the target of the magic was a continuous piece of material and was not thicker than . The spell only affected a single surface, so, for example, if there was a thin piece of tapestry on the other side of the object rendered transparent, the spell could not pierce it; a hollow-core door would also make the beneath the surface inefficient. The spell did not affect living creatures, only inanimate objects, as such, spilled blood or an ooze could serve as protection against the spell. Beneath the surface also did not allow light in dark or enclosed objects, so unless the caster could see in the dark, the visibility was limited when peeking inside a dark chest, for example.

This spell was simple magic, something Mordenkainen called "apprentice tools" and Elminster Aumar – "the stock-in-trade of us all." Because of its simplicity, it was often used by spellcasters to cheat at tabletop games or for thievery.

Components
Beneath the surface required verbal, somatic, and material components to cast. The material component was a -diameter piece of mica.

History
Beneath the surface was one of the spells that originated from one of two worlds: Toril or Oerth. The spell, along with several others, were exchanged between Elminster, Mordenkainen, and Rautheene of Oerth, during one of their visits to the home of Ed Greenwood on Earth.

## Berserk fury

Berserk fury was a spell used by clerics of Grolantor.

Effects
When cast, the caster immediately succumbed to a state of uncontrollable rage, unleashing a barrage of both encouraging and insulting shouts towards their allies. The aim was to incite their comrades to also succumb to a berserk state. Despite the shouts, the caster was still able to engage in attacks and defense.

The caster's allies affected by the spell immediately began echoing the priest's shouts, subjecting all other allies within range to the shouts. Once the number of affected individuals matched the caster's skill level, no additional allies were subjected to the spell's effects.

Those affected by the spell entered into a berserker state, becoming temporarily stronger and faster. While under the spell's influence, berserkers were compelled to engage in melee combat for as long as possible. They were unable to retreat, and once they have defeated all enemies, they turned their aggression towards their own allies. The spell expired on its own or when the caster ceased shouting.

Components
This spell only required a verbal component.

Appendix

## Beggars' Palaces

The Beggars' Palaces was the collective name for the two larger of the three ancient ziggurats that rose above the Old City slums of Port Nyanzaru, Chult.

Location
The main road into the Old City from the walled sections of Port Nyanzaru passed between the two Beggars' Palaces, with the gate to the Merchants' Ward just north of them and the Executioner's Run just south.

Structure
The Beggars' Palaces were crumbling ruins covered in vines. The shops and tenements of the Old City surrounded and climbed the Palaces, with bamboo structures densely crammed onto the ziggurat terraces. These shantytowns featured ramshackle markets near the base of the pyramids while the higher terraces were cleaner and less crowded than the lower ones. A wide wooden bridge connected the upper levels of the two pyramids.

Interior
Catacombs extended beneath the ziggurats, some of which could be accessed via a secret passage in the city's refuse pit. Some of these catacombs included trapped rooms and ritual chambers while others were sweltering mazes and crypts filled with the smells of rot and copper. These areas were adorned with carvings ranging from wavy lines and circular patterns (which appeared to date to the same time period as some of the tunnels beneath the city's Harbor Ward) to images of humans hunting and sacrificing other humans to dark gods.

Beneath the eastern ziggurat was a small tomb in which its architects had been interred, with walls adorned with carvings of Chultan laborers building the tomb. Access to this tomb was located at the ziggurat's western corner and was hidden behind a stack of crates in a ramshackle marketplace.

Activities
The Beggars' Palaces were associated with the beggar princes: criminals who wielded more power in the Old City than did Port Nyanzaru's merchant princes.

In times of attack, the interiors of the Beggars' Palaces served as places of shelter for the people of the Old City.

History
By the late 15 century DR, the civilization that had built the Beggars' Palaces was long-forgotten, and the catacombs beneath them had been sealed for many years. It was believed that the ancient kings who had commissioned the ziggurats had buried the architects alive to serve them in death.

While not traditionally considered to be one of the Beggars' Palaces, the third and smallest of the Old City's ziggurats came to be the base of operations for the beggar prince Pock-Marked Po.

Following the end of the death curse, a wight necromancer named Jaru Tasca took up residence in the catacombs beneath the Beggars' Palaces, and plotted to use a piece of the Cyst—a powerful necromantic mass of flesh—to transform Port Nyanzaru's residents and adventurers into undead. Her minions soon threatened the Old City.

## Beylia

Beylia was an acolyte in the Morningstar Haven, the temple to Lathander in the city of Westgate, in the late 14 century DR.

Description
In the, Beylia was a young woman, but her hair was streaked with grey, and crow's feet could be seen around her eyes.

History
By 1374 DR, Beylia had been an acolyte for thirteen years. Before that, she led a slaver's life in Westgate's criminal underbelly known as the Undergate. She served Kajeel, the so-called psion Master of Undergate, but abandoned her old life to serve the Morninglord. She retained a special token that allowed individuals passage to Kajeel through a guarded gate in Undergate. She was afraid of revealing that in front of her superiors at the Morningstar Haven, as she was supposed to dispose of the token by their orders when she joined. It was believed that she parted with the token one way or another, giving it to an adventurer who sought to discover the secrets of a cursed mask associated with the Night Masks in 1374 DR.

## Belakira

Ambassador Belakira was a Red Wizard and the political representative of the nation of Thay in the city of Ravens Bluff in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Belakira was pale skinned woman with piercing ice blue eyes and a largely shaved head, save for a long black ponytail that reached down to the small of her back. In keeping with Red Wizard tradition her head was decorated with intricate tattoos. These depicted a white dragon, as well as the holy symbols of Azuth and Mystra. Her face was often purposely void of expression.

Abilities
As a Red Wizard she knew a variety of spells. These included the following:
 chaos, cloudkill, death spell, Evard's black tentacles, flesh to stone, fumble, hold person, lightning bolt, magic missile, Melf's acid arrow, power word stun, read magic, slow, stoneskin, wall of force, and web.

Possessions
Belakira typically dressed in clothing with a red coloration, such as a formal red robe with gold hem that she would pair with a silver belt.

As a Red Wizard she owned a wide variety of magic items. In terms of magical apparel she owned bracers of defense, a cloak of protection, a ring of protection, a ring of invisibility, a necklace of adaptation, a robe of elemental resistance, and an amulet enspelled with a contingency teleport without error that would trigger in circumstances of mortal dangers. In terms of weaponry she had a wand of frost, a wand of petrification, and three beads of force.

History
As reported by the Ravens Bluff Trumpeter broadsheet in Uktar-Nightal of the, Ambassador Belakira filed an official complaint with the governing body of Ravens Bluff of an attack on the nation of Thay, perpetrated by an adventurer or a group of adventurers from the City of Ravens. The charges included a number of murdered Red Wizards of Thay. An eyewitness fingered a man that resembled Lord Lorien Darkarrow of Ravens Bluff, disguised as a Red Wizard, who was one of the accused perpetrators; however, the evidence was scant and unreliable. Ambassador Belakira gave a detailed interview about the accusation to Armond Arhesian of Ravens Bluff Trumpeter. The accusation was handled boldly by Mayor Thoden of Ravens Bluff, who presented a list of Thayan "Lords" who committed crimes in the City of Ravens, including murder, but were untouched by the law but to diplomatic immunity. On top of that, Lorien Darkarrow provided the law with an alibi. He was badly hurt by a black dragon's acid and teeth, allegedly, with the bodily wounds to prove it.

Ambassador Belakira appeared on the pages of Ravens Bluff Trumpeter once more in Mirtul of the . Journalist Guido of the Trumpeter reported on the murder of Dart Leftwin, one of the leaders of Port Disciples, a group that was said to have had affiliations with the Red Wizards. To this, Ambassador Belakira responded with a statement that these allegations were unproven and baseless as Thayan would never work with such "rabble," calming that if they indeed were Thayans, they were just renegades.

## Beliarge

Beliarge was a Zhentarim mage of the mid-14 century DR.

Description
At the time of his death, he wore a dark robe and many rings. He often sported a cruel smile.

Personality
To Fzoul Chembryl, Beliarge was ambitious and capable enough to be a threat. To foes at a disadvantage, he acted courteous but arrogant.

Abilities
He knew a spell of paralyzation that was powerful enough to hold even Storm Silverhand, and which would not be affected by anything he did, even if he ran her through in plain view.

History
In late, Fzoul Chembryl sent Beliarge to attack Tessaril's Tower and capture Shandril Shessair after the destruction of Iliph Thraun, urging him to attack while her spellfire was depleted. He materialized in the location shortly afterwards, striking Tessaril Winter and Storm Silverhand with a paralyzing spell of his own devising, powerful enough to hold the two of them; Shandril instead escaped into the Hidden House of Eveningstar. There, she ambushed him with a sword, managing to get a decisive kill before he could react.

## Belt of Bones

The Belt of Bones was a heavy enchanted belt created by the clergy of the Church of Myrkul some years before the late 13 century DR.

Description
The belt itself was made out of leather, set with metal plates and decorative hoops, all of which held human bones.

Powers
Belt of Bones was imbued with magic that allowed the wearer to cast the deadly circle of bones spell and the animate dead spell once per day each.

History
The original owner of the Belt of Bones was a dangerous warrior named Proud Keth who wore the belt into many battles, where the man massacred everyone in his way. Keth used the belt's magic on rare occasions when he was surrounded by enemies. Eventually, Proud Keth's enemies learned of his strategies and took him down once a for all using polearms and spears. The Belt of Bones was on Keth's body when the warrior was buried. However, how grave was soon desecrated and the magic belt – taken. The thieves ended up selling the Belt of Bones to the mage named Isanan from Urmlaspyr, a city in Sembia. The belt stayed with the wizard for many years until Isanan retired and sold the belt back to the Church of Myrkul and a very grateful cleric – Ben-Karad. Subsequently, Ben-Karad was said to had been killed in the of sewers of Waterdeep.

The belt's history was shrouded in mystery after Ben-Karad's death. However, in the, the Belt of Bones was believed to had reappeared in Icewind Dale during the conflict between the united Uthgardt and Reghedmen barbarians under the command of lord Wylfdene, in the barbarians' camp outside the town of Lonelywood.

## Ben-Karad

Ben-Karad was a priest of Myrkul who died years before the late 13 century DR.

History
Ben-Karad was one of the known owners of a unique enchanted Myrkulyte belt – the Belt of Bones. When the wizard from Urmlaspyr named Isanan retired, he found a buyer for the magic belt that served him for several years. Ben-Karad was overjoyed with being able to claim a Myrkulyte item for himself and gladly made the purchase. However, his life ended in the sewers of Waterdeep some years before the, and the Belt of Bones was though lost until it reappeared in Icewind Dale some time later.

## Belarla

Belarla was a pleasure queen in Zhentil Keep as of the mid–14 century DR, and also a Harper.

Personality
She knew all the dirty jokes in the business. Belarla had joined the Harpers for adventure.

Relationships
She was never far from Oelaerone, and she knew Mirt.

History
Belarla's work as a Harper and a pleasure queen of Zhentil Keep continued until the, when Mirt called upon her to aid Shandril Shessair after her first assault on the Citadel of the Raven. They followed her afterwards when she set out to attack the Wizards' Tower. When she was prevented from reaching that location by the patrols in the city, Belarla pacified Shandril using a sedative embedded in a napkin to prevent her from giving away their location, and helped fight off one wizard, for the trip across the sewers to Myrintara's home.

Myrintara later transported them to the Lonesome Tankard in Eveningstar. There, she argued in favor of Shandril striking again at the Zhents, rather that fleeing to Silverymoon. During the tussle with the forces of Fzoul Chembryl at the Wizards' Tower, Belarla was struck senseless early, taking her out of the fight. She survived the fight, however, and was healed by a kiss from Shandril and her spellfire, getting well enough to leave on her own power soon after.

Later that year, Belarla was drinking with Mirt when he and Asper were called magically to the aid of Shandril in the moments leading up to her death.Belarla is not present in the depiction of the events in the book; the web excerpts are taken to be corrections.

## Bellflower

Bellflowers were plants cultivated in elven communities. They were known for their scent and melodic sounds, but also served as a beautiful alarm system.

Description
Bellflowers chimed with a faint musical tinkling in the wind. They had different melodies according to the wind's direction and intensity.

Habitats
Bellflowers could be found in Evermeet, especially in the gardens around Moonstone Palace, as well as in other elven settlements.

Usage
Bellflower gardens were designed to catch the slightest breeze, making the plants tinkle constantly. Any intruders passing through the area created a disruption in the air flow, thus altering the melody. The change could go unnoticed by the intruders, but be as alarming as a trumpet to the heightened senses of elven guards.

## Besmer Altuth

Besmer Altuth was a criminal in Scornubel during the mid-14 century DR.

Possessions
Besmer came to possess a wand of fireballs that required no sorcerous knowledge to activate.

Relationships
Besmer worked in Scornubel under Belgon Bradaskor, the Master of Shadows.

Activities
Besmer's work for Belgon often earned him a few coppers or a silver coin.

History
Besmer attempted to jump Sharantyr in late, only to find her turning the tables on him. Rather than killing him, she demanded to be taken to be seen to the Master of Shadows, Belgon Bradaskor, introducing herself as Tessaril Winter instead of as herself. Besmer tried to lead her to an alley which she refused, so instead he offered the trip through either Rat Stair or Baluth's Hole. She chose Baluth's Hole. He was released only at the last moment, right before Sharantyr was able to meet Belgon; she gave him a handful of gold pieces for his trouble, more to make sure he was treated as a traitor than out of gratitude.

Days later, Besmer stole a unique wand of fireballs that anyone could wield, firing fireballs rather at will from a merchant, slaying him in the process. The man's guards chased after Besmer for a long time; he was not caught until he attempted to use the wand against the ghost of Evaereol Rathrane, who had been attracted by the wand's power. Rather than take damage from the fireball, the phantom drank of its charges, leaving Besmer defenseless against the guards who found him shortly after.

Rumors & Legends
Sharantyr was sure he would be killed for aiding her.

## Belgon Bradaskor

Belgon Bradaskor was a crime lord of Scornubel in the mid–14 century DR.

Possessions
Belgon's best horse was Flamewind.

Description
Belgon was quite obese.

Relationships
Belgon had a tall wife and a half-dozen daughters. People in his employ and familiar with his position included Tornar, Rendilar Bluthlock, Besmer Altuth, at least one bodyguard, and Nesger. He was an acquaintance of Tessaril Winter.

History
Belgon Bradaskor had been a part of the underworld in Scornubel since approximately . The last summer before late, almost certainly, he had become acquainted with Tessaril Winter, who had sworded Ulbegh. Between that time and the next year, she informed him that Ornrim had died, letting him deduce that the person in that position was an impersonator.

Late in the, Belgon was informed that Marlel's ambush for Shandril Shessair had failed, and that she'd be joining Orthil Voldovan's caravan. He arranged for Rendilar Bluthlock to hire some undesirables from the local criminal element, which would ensure their elimination. Rendilar would later die attacking the caravan, almost certainly implying the thugs were hired to attack the caravan as well.

Soon after, Lhaeo sent Sharantyr to find Shandril, and Belgon was named as the first person to seek out in order to find her. She began by luring in Besmer Altuth, a criminal, who attempted to rob her; after turning the tables on him, she force him to show her the way to Belgon.

Upon meeting him, Sharantyr managed to learn from him that Shandril had left with Orthil Voldovan's caravan. He had a horse and rations delivered to her so she could catch up as soon as possible, but after she left, he sent Tornar after her, with orders to kill her. He was informed some time after of Shandril's immolation and the caravan's destruction by Nesger. Tornar had later agreed to tell him he'd managed to slay Sharantyr, though he hadn't.

He finally became king at age 16 in the, having taken the regnal name Belhinn I.

## Beldimarr

Beldimarr was a Harper operating as a caravan driver in the Sword Coast during the mid-14 century DR.

Appearance
Beldimarr had a snakelike scar down his right temple all the way to his neck and hairy chest. He had relatively poor dental hygiene.

Relationships
As of, Tessaril Winter was aware of Arauntar and Beldimarr's status as Harpers. Both of them worked for Orthil Voldovan, who wasn't a Harper himself. She, too, was an acquaintance of his'. Arauntar and Beldimarr had a pact to kill each other if the other was to ever be mortally wounded and left in agony.

History
In, Tessaril Winter instructed Shandril Shessair and Narm Tamaraith to join Voldovan's caravan to make the road from Scornubel to Waterdeep, telling them also that Beldimarr and Arauntar, of his men, were Harpers.

The two departed Scornubel alongside Orthil Voldovan with a caravan of thirty-two wagons, plus two "ready-wagons", one wagon for strongboxes and small cargo, and a cook wagon. Orthil was pleased when Shandril and Narm asked to disguise themselves as guards, which they did during the departure from Scornubel; Arauntar and Beldimarr helped Sharantyr disguise herself better as one of Voldovan's men, which were all men. However, even from the start, one of his guards, Storstil, was slain almost as soon as they left Scornubel, and spells in the wagons killed two people. He quickly began to suspect Shandril and Narm at that point, though he had eyewitnesses that placed them away from the murder scenes. Arauntar took forward scouting position, while Orthil tasked Beldimarr, who remained behind, with searching the dead Storstil. Orthil claimed to be traveling with one mage, but believe there were a dozen more amongst the travelers; rather than find the culprit, he priorized making it to Face Crag before nightfall, which made for a better defensible position. Along the way, they were attacked before making it to the crag.

During the ambush, Voldovan tied up Narm, believing him to have gone out of line by trying to go find Shandril, abandoning his post, rather than protecting the part of the caravan where he had been assigned. Voldovan later approached Shandril, who was with Thorst; he called on her to find some of his healing accoutrements, and ordered Arauntar and Beldimarr, one of which had slain two bandits during the ambush, to drive Thorst's wagon. Arauntar and Beldimarr later checked on the wagons; one of the wagons they checked was Haransau Olimer's; the man on whom they checked up there was actually Marlel, who had impersonated the blandreth seller.

On a morning later during the trip, Orthil came to Shandril to demand why she wasn't up. During their conversation Arauntar and Beldimarr turned up and said how they'd arranged for some goods to be moved to the abandoned wagons, still in better shape; Shandril resolved she and Narm would drive on the same wagon, which Beldimarr approved of, claiming they'd be easier to keep track of. During that morning, Arauntar and Beldimarr inspected the wagon belonging to Mhegras and Sabran, twice; the second time, they checked on the secret floor they had spotted the first time. Voldovan was summoned for that. The master of the caravan brought along Shandril and Narm, and the latter checked the contraband horse barding for magic; finding nothing of interest, they left.

After a later ambush, Orthil Voldovan personally sought out Shandril and Narm, and took them to Arauntar and Beldimarr. The latter was about to fulfill a pact with Arauntar, to dispatch him painlessly if mortally wounded; instead, Shandril used her spellfire to heal him. She later used her power to heal Beldimarr; she found the limit of her strength there. Voldovan called afterwards on everyone to hurry to Orcskull Rise. At Orsckull Rise, Arauntar briefly chatted with Shandril.  The night was itself uneventful.

During the next ambush, after Orcskull Rise, Arauntar was helped in attacking Thoadrin's forces by Shandril. She heard Beldimarr's response to the spell that Aumlar Chauntoun called down, which was rather crude.

Arauntar and Beldimarr killed Mhegras and Sabran later, while the latter two spied on Shandril. Arauntar later carried Shandril back to the caravan, and together with Beldimarr, transferred Narm and Shandril to a wagon which they crammed with spare axles and wheels. Arauntar had slain Mhegras, while Beldimarr had slain Sabran; unknown to them, the two had been raised thanks to a contingent spell. Closing on Triel, in sight of Duskview house, Beldimarr related to Shandril and Narm about Triel. At Duskview house, Orthil Voldovan and Beldimarr met with herald Stormshield for hiring new sellswords as guards. Briefly, Beldimarr was distracted by another Harper he'd never seen telling him the Three Laws would soon apply on every city; Orthil, after that moment, seemed to have difficulty recognizing Beldimarr, and spoke more stiffly.

During the night after leaving Triel, Arauntar and Beldimarr had to protect Shandril and Narm again, as a magic-drinking entity, Evaereol Rathrane, had the caravan in its influence and was blocking both her spellfire and his spells. Sharantyr reached the caravan at this point, and Arauntar took her to be another hunter after spellfire. Only Shandril's intervention prevented them from killing each other; Sharantyr, after that point, said that the man pretending to be Orthil Voldovan wasn't. Beldimarr was then mortally wounded; when Shandril attempted to heal him, she failed, and instead slew him.

## Benante's Butchery

Benante's Butchery was a small but renowned butcher shop in the enclave of Eileanar, owned and operated by the eponymous Benante.

Services
Benante supplied fine meats to residents of Karsus within a 20-block radius, enjoying the financial support of his wife Acciajuoli that allowed him to pursue the highest quality without worrying about quantity. Cattle and sheep were slaughtered using a portal to the Negative Energy plane that painlessly drained their life force. After the carcasses hung and drained for three days, the priest Franchvail used her divine magics to bless and purify the meats of any parasites or diseases before it was cut to the customer's specifications.

## Benzoate Glycol

Benzoate Glycol was a rather dimwitted and extremely handsome adventurer in the city of Ravens Bluff in the 14 century DR.

Activities
Over his years of adventuring, Benzoate acquired membership in the city of Ravens Bluff's Clerical Circle, its Wizards' Guild, its Bards' Guild, its Navy, and its knightly order the Golden Roosters.

History
Sometime in the late 14 century DR, while the war with Myrkyssa Jelan's forces was raging, Benzoate proposed marriage to the Lliiran priestess Tellasi Salilei while at the House of Thud. When later pressed by the Ravens Bluff Trumpeter to comment on these rumors he reacted with surprise, stating, "I did? How about that!" Meanwhile, the paper was unable to reach Tellasi to comment on the matter.

Later, Benzoate stayed a night at the Seven Tigers Inn before leaving the city entirely to travel south alongside Lorien Darkarrow in a hunt for the rogue mammoth Thundering Death.

Following the end of the war, Benzoate was one of many adventurers who supported the A.V.O.W. shelter. Later he bought a spaniel breed dog from the pet-store You Can Buy Love, unaware that it was actually a rat that had been transformed by the owners with a wand of polymorph. And he attended the housewarming party that was held for Briar Oldhill to celebrate the opening of his chapel, Briar's Patch.

Eventually Benzoate received a promotion in the Navy, rising to the rank of Lieutenant Commander, and in that same year rose to the rank of Golden Crown Rooster within the Knights of the Golden Rooster.

Appendix
Background
Benzoate was a player-character who participated in the Living City RPGA events and adventures. He was played by Jean Rabe from Wisconsin.

## Berry bowl

Berry bowls were a simple type of gnomish highsunfeat dessert from the city of Ravens Bluff in the Vast.

Description
Berry bowls were just that – bowls filled with a variety of berries, depending on what was in season. The berries were drizzled with sugar sauce.

History
Berry bowls were the only alternative to the chocolate crab cakes at a gnomish out-of-the way café in Ravens Bluff called Le PoTeete Café in the late 14 century DR. More adventurous customers preferred strange but delicious chocolate crab cakes.

## Beehive Goods and Wares

Beehive Goods and Wares was a general store located on Stormshore Street in the Lower City of Baldur's Gate, run by Oliver Tefoco in the late 15 century DR.

Location

Beehive Goods and Wares was located in a small two-storied building somewhere roughly the southeast of The Blushing Mermaid in the Lower City and just south of the Crimson Draughts shoppe. The shop's entrances were located under an arch formed by the building's second floor that stretched north over the street to another exit located on the second floor, used by the Tefoco and living quarters. The sign that hung above the entrance depicted a beehive surrounded by bottles of wine and crates. The shop itself was on the building's first floor, with the room filled with cupboards and shelves of goods that included food, potions, wines, and some general miscellany.

History
In the, Beehive Goods and Wares was run by Oliver Tefoco and was home to his spouse Kroyce Tefoco and two youngest children–Hemjax and Maggie. The family's older children lived in other parts of the city and traveled with merchant caravans along the Sword Coast North. When the cultist army of The Absolute targeted the Gate and began a march to attack it, Beehive Goods and Wares became the home to Oliver only as he sent his spouse and children to their family in Beregost, out of harm's way. Beehive remained abuzz with business through the tumult of the coming war. During that time, Notale's Hostel that sheltered refugees, asked Oliver Tefoco for any supplies he would be willing to donate. In turn, Beehive Goods and Wares's own supplier–Durinbold & Oathoon, Provisoners, suspended deliveries of food and dry goods until trade caravans could enter the Gate.

## Belt of the witch king

The belt of the witch king was an enchanted belt that once belonged to Zhengyi the Witch-King.This item's lore is established in the Living Forgotten Realms adventure, Tyranny's Perilous Bastion. Note that the item backstory provided in Adventurer's Vault 2 is different, and is non-canon for the Forgotten Realms.

Description
The belt was a series of small obsidian spheres charged with arcane power, which were each connected to the next by a small silver link of chain.

Powers
The belt fortified the wearer's endurance. If its wearer was an arcane spellcaster, it could leverage their magical power to heal them if they became seriously injured.

History
The belt of the witch king was kept in a secret compartment in the back of Zhengyi's throne within Castle Perilous, alongside other treasures. It remained there for over a century after Zhengyi's defeat at the hands of Gareth Dragonsbane.

Bearclaw was a light-skinned gnomish woman with brown eyes and short bright-red hair. She wore barbarian hides and hid her face behind a crimson mask. The gnome used a massive axe in battle.

In the, the opportunistic and amoral mercenary Aradin Beno set out to find the Nightsong for the mage Lorroakan. ## Before Its Time

Before Its Time is a one round adventure module set in the Living City organized play program. It was published in 1999, and is intended for adventurers of all levels.

Synopsis
After attending a grand opening party at the Tavern of the Garden in Ravens Bluff, a group of heroes of the Living City was approached by a young woman – the proprietor. Shauna Morden revealed to the group that she recently discovered a winery that her late father owned, and she needed hirelings to visit the property, find out about its state, and connect with the winery's keepers, if any. The group traveled up the Fire River aboard The Topside to the town of Dragon Falls, the nearest settlement to the winery. After interviewing locals and the renowned druid Keldar of the Forest, the heroes met the winery's new owner, a secret maedar – Vorlon, who took over the winery, alongside his wife- medusa Samanthiss. The duo found the winery almost a decade before that, taking it from a young Chauntean. After some investigation, the group discovered the true nature of the new owners and how they claimed the winery; however, the media and her husband lacked malice and truly loved winemaking. Subsequently, the heroes resolved the ownership via one of several possible ways: they likely connected Vorlon and Shauna Morden, making for a fruitful business. Another possibility was that heroes ended the serpentine winemaker and returned the winery to Larn, the young Chauntean who spent a decade petrified.

Belsarth was a highknight.

Together with Lorton Ironstone, Belsarth Hawkblade was part of an ambush waiting for Elminster Aumar when the latter tried to sneak into Castle Obarskyr in Suzail sometime after Mirtul 26 of the . ## Belgryn Murenstur

Belgryn Murenstur was a merchant of Cormyr during the late 15 century DR.

Activities
He was the proprietor of Murenstur's Imported Vintages.

History
One night in Suzail, Belgryn Murenstur was accosted by the blueflame ghosts Relve Langral and Treth Halonter, who asked him if he'd seen Seszgar Huntcrown recently. Belgryn volunteered that the man was at the Bold Archer, a tavern; the ghosts insisted he lead the way, despite his protests he was going home. Seeing their swords were slick with blood and there were bodies not far, he acceded to their demands.

He then led them to the Bold Archer; under duress, but also under the evil archmage Manshoon's remote mental influence, he reconnoited for them, under duress, that Seszgar Huntcrown had at least six bodyguards, plus many other nobles had brought a retinue. He was thanked; Treth almost slew him, but Relve reminded him they needed to leave a witness.

## Belltoll Street

Belltoll Street was a slightly curved street that began on the The Wide, ran west and ended at a moot in front of the home of sage Ragefast in the Upper City of Baldur's Gate.

Location
Belltoll Street began at one of the western enrages into The Wide, where the Hlath Estate stood, on the edge of the Temples District. From there, the street cut through the entire Temples District, passing between the Portyr Estate and the High Hall, crossing Windspell Street where the Vanthampur Villa and the Helm and Cloak stood. Belltoll Street continued moving west into the Manorborn district, crossing Sornbanner Street, slightly curving north, and passing the Lady's Hall temple, until reaching the moot in front of Ragefast's manse.

## Beholder dictionary

A list of words and phrases from the Quevquel beholder language.

B
 binphallkahr
 a balldozer device.

 bombardment
 an artistic performance using eyestalk rays.

D
 diohurr
 "spasms." A degenerative beholder disease encountered in old-age.

 The Dropping
 a contest that determines the rulership of a city.

E
 edorakk
 "mania." A congenital mental disorder that causes odd behaviour in beholders.

 evil eyes
 beholders that have unorthodox inherent powers in their eyes, considered offensive and profane by other beholders.

H
 hakvatt
 "befouler." An insulting term for a beholder of a different breed that has given birth.

 holvatt
 "contributor." A respectful term for a beholder of the same breed that has given birth.

I
 iolvatt
 "old meat." An insulting title used for a beholder of a different breed that is more than 90 years old.

 ixhmarn
 "Parade." The ritualized inspection of a newly-born true-bred beholder.

K
 kakwatt
 "punk." A dismissive term for a young beholder.

 kawahak kru
 "spawner-boss." The term for a hive mother.

 Kghrix
 "hall of heroes." A place where beholders gather to tell stories.

M
 malohurr
 "meat-rot." A case of mild food poisoning caused by eating rotting or incompatible food.

 mouthpick
 a hand-replacement device used for tools and weapons.

S
 sarruin
 "little-kin." Refers to small beholderkin such as gazers. Derived from the Uibilaqthraxx word lurl'uk'lok'lahassarruin.

 spellstalk
 a spellcasting beholder's eyestalk that has been repurposed for storing readied arcane spells.

T
 tiusium
 the lighter-than-air gas inside a beholder that enables its flight.

W
 washatt
 "elder." A title of respect used for a beholder of the same breed that is more than 90 years old.

## Beguiling rebuke

Beguiling rebuke was a unique enchantment charm spell from the sixth circle of magic used in the late 15 century DR.

Effects
The beguiling rebuke spell was cast as a reaction, mere moments after the caster was attacked. Once activated, the rebuked assailant and their nearby allies had a nigh chance of temporarily becoming beguiled. This charming effect, if successful, stopped the affected creatures from attacking the caster for twelve seconds. This magical rebuke had the range of . The effects of beguiling rebuke could not be strengthened by a casting at a higher proficiency level.

History
This unique sell was known and was used by the cambion spawn of Mephistopheles, Raphael during the Absolute crisis of the, were he to stand against the Absolute Adversaries during their foray into the House of Hope in Avernus.

## Beholder graft

Beholder grafts were pieces or parts of beholders that were grafted onto another creature.

Description
These grafts were developed by bizarre cults who idolized beholders and wished to emulate them. However, beholders believed the practice to be abhorrent, and viewed it as a defilement of their "perfect" forms.

Beholder grafts did not radiate magic however they conferred protection against gas spores, who naturally avoided grafted individuals as they would a beholderkin.

Creation
Attaching beholder parts to another creature was a magical rite that was quite painful for the recipient. To implant the graft required familiarity with medicine and specialized knowledge of how to graft flesh and craft muscle. If implanting or grafting a beholder's eye, the process also required access to the spell(s) that corresponded to the eye's power. The grafts could not be scavenged or removed from a creature without being destroyed.

Users
These grafts were created by beholder cultists for implantation into their own members in an effort to attain power. These cults tended to arise among humans, goblinoids, or subterranean creatures such as derro, kobolds, or troglodytes.

Notable Beholder Grafts
Crown of eyes
Six small eyes were implanted around the circumference of the recipient's skull, conferring limited 360-degree vision.
Eye stalk
A beholder's eye stalk was grafted onto the recipient's head and connected to their nervous system. Any given stalk could only produce one kind of beholder eye ray, as determined at the time of grafting.
Gazing eye
One of the recipient's eyes was replaced with a beholder's eye that had the innate power to use a few different beholder gaze attacks (cause fear, charm person, flesh to stone, or sleep).
Plated skin
Bony plates akin to those of a beholder's were grafted over the recipient's vulnerable areas to provide permanent armor.
Replacement eye
One of the recipient's eyes was replaced with a beholder's eye that could produce one kind of beholder eye ray, as determined at the time of grafting.
Third eye
An eye was implanted into the middle of the recipient's forehead that acted as a miniaturized version of a beholder's central eye, although it could only be activated three times each day.

Notable Owners
 Beldar Roaringhorn, a Waterdhavian nobleman and member of a group called Amalgamation during .
 Golskyn, the leader of Amalgamation during 1370 DR.

Appearances

## Benji (ghoul)

Benji was the Principal Sniffer, a ghoul, kept by Lucretious, the proprietress of of the Circus of the Last Days in the late 15 century DR.
Description
Benji appeared to be an ordinary blood-splattered leather-skinned undead ghoul with limited intelligence and a docile nature, thanks to being controlled by the Circus of the Last Days. The ghoul was dressed in a colorful jester-like garb.
History
In the, Benji could be found at the gate of Lucretious's circus in Rivington of Baldur's Gate, where the extra-planar troupe performed for a short period of time, before moving on to another world. In lieu of recently surging murders in Baldur's Gate, Benji was tasked with sniffing out blood on every guest entering the circus. The ghoul was supervised by Klaus, the Assistant Sniffer. One of the sniffees was the commoner man named Montgomery, who brought his son to see the famed Dribbles the clown. Even though the father and the son both were clean, the boy was terrified of the blood-covered jester ghoul and refused to enter the circus.

## Bereft

Berefts were wielders of truespeak who sought to wield and safeguard the power of the Word of Unmaking, a single word that held the power to unravel all of creation.

Abilities
It was necessary to use a spell, the ritual of renaming, on a prospective bereft; the bereft's new name was always made as obscure as possible. Discovering a bereft's personal truename was an inordinate challenge.

Syllable by syllable, berefts slowly assembled the Word of Unmaking; a bereft that had just started would only know the first syllable, the syllable of detachment, which made the target of its utterance deeply maladroit. The second syllable came next; this, the syllable of affliction, temporarily erased one of the target's senses. The third syllable, the syllable of exile, allowed them to evict their target from reality for a few seconds; the fourth syllable, when mastered, let them partly unmake and tear the body of one target, dealing severe damage. The final syllable, on its own, dealt the sort of lifeforce drain often seen dealt by powerful undead.

By speaking all the syllables together, a bereft who had mastered them all could speak a word of unmaking. Aimed at a dead person within range, it made the subject impossible to raise or resurrect in its present state. It required a ritual of renaming, like the one the bereft had gone through, to make them once more possible to raise, and even then only a true resurrection would work anymore.

Relationships
Berefts went as far as erasing their own true name to stay obscure, and obscure indeed they were; even among truenamers bereft were at the far fringe. It was not unthinkable for berefts to erase even their own names from their own mind, as well as that of all who ever knew them, and strike them from no end of records, using magic if necessary. The few who knew what they were in spite of the lengths to which they went to erase all knowledge of themselves, tended to be unfriendly somewhat.

The bereft were commonly of the Church of Shar.

## Belt of Valindra's guard

Belts of Valindra's guard were enchanted belts that were created to be boons for Valindra Shadowmantle's most powerful minions in the late 15 century DR.

Powers
The wearers of these belts received boosts to their vitality, accuracy, intelligence, charisma, and ability to land lucky devastating strikes. When an amulet of Valindra's favor was worn together with a belt of Valindra's guard and a shard of Valindra's crown, the belt greatly fortified the wearer's resistance to enthralling and controlling spells and, in turn, strengthened the wearer's own such spells.

History
These belts were given out to the most powerful of Valindra Shadowmantle's minions as a sign of favor. After Valindra's defeat at the hands of the defenders of Neverwinter in the late 15 century DR, some of these belts were recovered from the villains' bodies.

## Beads of brewing

Beads of brewing were enchanted pellets created to enhance the quality and longevity of any brewed alcoholic drinks.

Description
Beads of brewing showed no signs of possessing any magic and could easily be confused with mundane beads.

Powers
To utilize the magic within the beads of brewing, one had to simply drop one or two pellets into a vat during the brewing process. No more than two beads could be used at the same time in a vat of up to a thousand gallons. These beads dramatically improved the beer or ale's color, flavor, smell, and texture and quadrupled the entire batch's shelf-life as well as quadrupled the distance the brews could be exported without a loss of quality and spoilage.
Two types of beads of brewing existed. The beads of a light brown color boosted the vat's quality by twenty-five percent, and the dark brown beads improved it by fifty percent.

History
By the mid-14 century DR, very few knew how to create such magic items. Only the most trusted allies of the Brewers' Guild, like Ashakar Piscelene of Ashakar's Accessories in Ravens Bluff, were entrusted with the secret. Ashakar and fellow trusted mages created the beads exclusively for the members of the guild and only for breweries in the city of Ravens Bluffs and within territories controlled by it. Though such exclusivity, the Brewers' Guild sought to make Ravens Bluff the beer-brewing center of Faerûn.

## Belvedere Huntsilver

Belvedere Huntsilver was a Cormyte nobleman of House Huntsilver in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Belvedere was an aged human man with white hair and muttonchops. He carried a cane with a large knob at the end.

Personality

Belvedere was a gruff and severe man, concerned heavily with the reputation of House Huntsilver, but admitting that Oriphaun's health came first, as did fighting the enemies of Cormyr.

History

In the late 15 century DR, Belvedere Huntsilver, accompanied by the footman Antone Corluk, came to Eveningstar to find his wayward nephew Oriphaun Huntsilver, who had taken up the lifestyle of a treasure hunter and adventurer. He hired a team of competent-seeming adventurers to find and help his nephew, despairing as they followed Oriphaun through Wheloon Prison, the troubles in Druid's Deep, the battles on the High Road, and finally into the Storm Horns where Oriphaun clashed with Archmage Amskar.
