## Belundrar

Belundrar "the Bear" was a Baron of Galadorna during the mid–8 century DR.

Personality
He was the most loyal baron of King Baerimgrim, and the most law-abiding of them.

Description
He had hairy hands, a beard, and was huge. His eyes were green, with bushy brows, and his nose was jutting, but battered.

Relationships
He was older than the other barons, and belonged to the oldest and largest noble house in Galadorna. The king viewed him as the best of a bad lot.

Possessions
During the meeting about King Baerimgrim's succession, there were many daggers at his belt.

History
Baron Belundrar provided additional armsmen for King Baerimgrim during the ailing man's moot to determine succession in the . Though he had issues with Baron Hothal's dealings with Laothkund, he was willing to support that, if it meant a solid bid for the throne. He was then blindsided by Baron Maethor backing Baron Tholone's bid for the throne, and the rest of the barons coalescing around that one.

A spell then killed Tholone and the king; after Nadrathen tried to make his own bid for the throne, Belundrar attacked him, and was killed by the mage just as quickly.

## Begoas

Begoas the Inquisitive was a handsome human necromancer from the Old Empires region and the first Minister of Necromancy in the city of Ravens Bluff's Ministry of Art in the 14 century DR.

Personality
Begoas was a soft-spoken individual, prone to giving wise advice. He had a deep fascination with human anatomy, a fascination that some believed had no boundaries. When it came to necromancy, Begoas was very outspoken against the prejudice of Ravens Bluff's populace, saying that his study of that school of magic was done for the purpose of opposing those who would use it for evil purposes, though many Ravenians didn't believe his protestations.

History
Begoas was born somewhere in the Old Empires region, and was speculated to be either Mulhorandi or Untheri.

Begoas's time in the Ministry of Art began with its formation by Lord Mayor Charles Oliver O'Kane in the, shortly before the month of Flamerule. During those early days, public opposition towards the formation of the governing body included criticism of the inclusion of a representative for the necromancy school of magic, arguing that it was inherently evil.

In Ches of the, the Ministry of Art and Wizards Guild were unified; the two wizardly organizations allied their functions and leadership under a new name: the Guild of Magical Arts. The Ravens Bluff Trumpeter reported the unification was a desperate act caused by the Three Women War taxing the magic and resources of both organizations. Nevertheless, Begoas and his fellow Ministers, along with the ranking members of the Wizards Guild, unanimously approved of the merger. During that time, Begoas coached and led Wizards Guild's members that specialized necromancy.

However, after only two months of operation, the Guild of Magical Arts dissolved in Tarsakh. The Ravens Bluff Trumpeter reported ministers had begun leaving the joint organization to reform the Ministry of Art after quickly becoming disillusioned with Alcides Von Tighe's leadership. Those seats that remained with the Guild of Magical Arts included Begoas's seat and the seat for wild magic. However, the wild magic seat's position was a forced one, as Minister Glora Vano was on the run after being accused of treason for aiding Myrkyssa Jelan. Both organizations were unsure of how to proceed, and no plans for recruiting new Ministers were made.

By Mirtul, 1370 DR, Begoas the Inquisitive was the only member who'd returned to the Ministry of Art. Back in the seat of necromancy, Begoas headed an investigation and search for the traitor Glora Vano. His investigation uncovered an ancient tomb that was believed to be the ex-Minister's hideout. The Ministry of Art disclosed the tomb's location to any and all adventurers willing to bring Glora Vano to justice.

## Beldrune

Beldrune was a sage of the 8 century DR. He was sometimes an actor, too.

Personality
Tabarast and Beldrune sometimes got into sesquipedalian talk, using long or old words. Tabarast was especially bad about this when he was mad. The two were known to flirt with each other.

Beldrune did not believe the gods handed down spells, but he was more reverent than Tabarast. He could sure drink a lot.

Beldrune used a worry stone to calm himself down sometimes. He was sensitive about his manner of dress.

Activities
As of, they had spent years at Moonshorn Tower attempting to recover old magical lore.

In, they were selling entry to their magic classes for a gold piece per attendant.

Description
Beldrune had some hair, and was dressed in a scarlet silk tunic that was adorned with cloth-of-gold dragons with emeralds for eyes. He also described himself as overweight.

Abilities
He wasn't a great rider.

Beldrune was able to cast some manner of spell that allowed him to scry on distant lands.

Possessions
Beldrune had a worry stone.

Relationships
Tabarast and Beldrune had long worked together; Tabarast was the elder of the two.

Beldrune favored one of his students, Maglast.

History
Beldrune had once acted in a performance of "The Stormy Knight", where he played the Second Undergardener. According to Tabarast, he'd probably never been on an adventure, though Tabarast had known adventure. The two of them had been to Ripplestones at some point prior to .

As of the, Beldrune and Tabarast had been in Moonshorn Tower for some time, studying the texts there. They had agreed not to broach the subject of Chosen of Mystra, but did so anyway, speculating idly as to the signs claimed by the priests of Mystra's Mantle in Haramettur that Elminster Aumar was once again walking the lands. They had been at this task for years.

By the spring of that year, Tabarast and Beldrune noticed a stranger walking into Moonshorn; unknown to them, this was the Elminster they'd heard about. Though they turned away from the stranger until he left, there were burning letters left in the documents he read; they took those as a sign of Mystra's direct intervention, and decided to pursue Elminster, and be witnesses in his wake. They rode after him on mules. On the way, they were soon passed by Ilbryn Starym, who was also in pursuit of Elminster.

They reached the Castle of the Lady, where Elminster Aumar apprenticed under the sorceress Dasumia at some point before the, by which point they were already observing the comings and goings of the two mages from one or more hidden positions near the castle. They continued to spy on the castle until Beldrune scried on Nethra, guided by a dream that he considered an omen of Mystra, learning of the coronation of Dasumia and Elminster's new position as the Court Mage almost as soon as that event happened. Before they left, they clashed with Ilbryn Starym and Tabarast humbled him in spell-battle, but they did share with him the whereabouts of Elminster. They remained in Nethra afterwards; during the last day that Dasumia and Elminster remained in town, they were at the Goblet of Shadows, a local tavern. They drank a lot there.

By, they had abandoned their Elminster search, and had settled somewhere near Starmantle. There, one day, Tabarast came across a rejuvenation spell in an empty page of his spellbook; Beldrune attested to feeling rejuvenated by it. The two of them had been teachers, for quite some time at that point, until they were commissioned by a Harper, secretly the god Azuth, to hunt after Elminster, whom he was seeking.

They made a short stop for food at the Fair Maid, a tavern in Ripplestones, town where they nearly rode down Baerdagh, a local elderly man, and then were joined by Caladaster Daermree, another local elderly man and former wizard, who agreed to guide them to nearby ruins where he was almost certain Elminster would turn up. The man led them to Karsus' Keep, where they came across Elminster just as the Slayer, the ghost of the Netherese sorceress Saeraede Lyonora, was beginning to feed on him. The Harper revealed himself to be Azuth, and hurled deadly spells at Elminster in an effort to dislodge Saeraede from him. Just as Azuth warned him that if she stayed on him, he'd be doomed, five Dreadspells of Shar blasted the chamber with a cavalcade of deadly staves from a hole in the ceiling.

Azuth protected the three wizards from the destruction that ensued. Then, upon the death of Saeraede, who was struck down by Ilbryn Starym after he had burst from hiding to attack, Azuth held back the explosion that resulted from Saeraede's death throes. Azuth then vanished together with Elminster, leaving the three mages awed and briefly bewildered. They rambled shortly, and then found among the rubble three gowns that Saeraede had stolen previously.

Afterwards, they headed  for the Fair Maid, where they had some drinks, showed around the dresses, and talked about their latest adventure. Beldrune embellished heavily, claiming that it was their spells that had saved the day and that Azuth had told them so. Tabarast added that Azuth had told them they'd earned the magical robes they were showing off, though Azuth had said nothing of the sort. Sharindala, a local undead sorceress, later joined them at the tavern briefly, before departing with Caladaster, but said everything they'd said about Azuth was true.

## Bell-Breakers

Bell-Breakers was a criminal organization, the muscle part of the Guild of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

History
During the Absolute crisis of the, the newly-risen Kingpin named Stone Lord, started a guild war against the Guild, strategically taking out the Guild's agents, kingpins, and taking over their territory. The Stone Lord was a thrall of the Cult of the Absolute, and as a part of the guild war, his men targeted gangs and smaller organizations under the Guild's rule. Bell-Breakers were among those hit by Stone Lord's men, however, their fate was unconfirmed. The Guild tasked Fruward the Nail with investigation shortly before the Zhentarim attempted to take over the Guild's operations in Baldur's Gate.

Fruward's report was a letter attesting to the Bell-Breakers' demise. The group and the belfry it was nesting in atop a temple in Heapside were obliterated by the Stone Lord.

History
Around the same time as the death curse, Draza was arrested for theft and sentenced to Executioner's Run. This led a distraught Belym to try and persuade anyone in authority that his husband had been mistakenly convicted without a proper trial, but he was unable to convince anyone of this. Just as Draza was about to be thrown into the Run with a pair of velociraptors, Belym turned to adventurers, offering them all of his meager savings to help or protect Draza from the sidelines.

## Belvyn's House of Good Cheer

Belvyn's House of Good Cheer was a combined festhall, all-faiths temple, and rental hall in the settlement of Bargewright Inn in the Savage Frontier in the mid-to-late 14 century DR. It was owned and rented out by Belvyn.

Location
It stood in the upper part of Bargewright Inn, on the central northern side between Tabra's and Shondrin's Packsack of Plenty on the street known as the Rise.

Structure
The building was a tall and slender hall, with a steep roof. The layout was simple and open. Along its high crossbeams ran catwalk galleries.

Services
The hall was available to hire for 25 gp for a night. Typically, it was used as either a festhall or a temple, as incongruous as those might seem. For the former, the hall was hired for revels by a variety of organizations, usually mercantile leagues, the guilds of Waterdeep getting out of the city, and adventuring companies passing through and look to celebrate some victory. Such groups regularly hired many of the escorts from the neighboring Tabra's for the evening.

If hired as temple, then worshipers were provided with a plain altar and braziers if required. Any non-violent religion was permitted, as well as those of Helm and Tempus, while that of Malar was expressed excluded.

Free of charge, one could also hold a funeral here.

Rumors & Legends
A recurring rumor in the area claimed that, hidden somewhere within or beneath the House of Good Cheer, were many chests filled with gold pieces. This was easily discredited, however: the building had no hiding places and not an errant copper piece was ever turned up.

## Bell of warning

A bell of warning was a type of small magical bell from the land of Kara-Tur that could detect the presence of anyone approaching it covertly.

Description
These bells measured  in length and  in diameter.

Powers
Activating a bell of warning required hanging it, such as upon a branch or rafter, and then speaking its command word is spoken. Each activation used up a magical charge, with each bell having upwards of 30 charges, and lasted for around 3 to 18 hours.

While activate a bell of warning would ring whenever any creature came within  from either the Astral plane or Ethereal plane. It likewise would sound from any creatures that were invisible or attempting to move stealthily or silently. This ringing would then persist until a bell of warning either deactivated or the detected presence moved outside of its effective radius.

Notable Owners
 Ayana No-Shui, a powerful wu-jen.
 The Horseshoe Temple in the Quoya Desert had a bell of warning amongst its collection of magical instruments. When the temple was later overran by oni in 1358 DR, it was amongst the items that survived.
 Lui-hsia Chih, a leader of the Many-Hued Peacock Society.

History
Around  to early, a group of bakemono took up residence in the long abandoned Lihat Observatory, believing it held unusual weaponry inside. As part of establishing defenses within the observatory, the bakemono placed  bell of warning in the anteroom to alert them of any intruders.No date is given for Ronin Challenge, nor its companion adventure Test of the Samurai. However, it includes Kara-Turan characters from Hall of Heroes, which is dated to 1358 DR, and is set after the unfinished events detailed there. In particular, it seems at least "about a year" later for the Earth Spider. On the other hand, it includes characters featured in Dragonwall, which is dated to 1359/1360 DR, which clearly occurs later for them. In particular, Ting Mei Wan was recently made a minister in Ronin Challenge and has been minister for two years by the time of Dragonwall. These narrow the main events of Ronin Challenge to late 1358 DR or early 1359 DR.

## Bertrand Lucere

Bertrand Lucere was a merchant in Luskan during the late 15 century DR.

History
A merchant noted for his business ethics, Bertrand had worked his way up building a business that traded in lumber and iron. In the 1480s DR, however, he moved into specialty goods like rare herbs and minerals with alchemical properties used in potion-making. He met often with many of the town's merchants.

He had a daughter, Indra Lucere, to whom he left a substantial inheritance.

Some time around 1490 DR, Bertrand was murdered, his throat slit, in the entry hall of his home. He was found by his daughter Indra. A select few valuables of his most recent shipment, among them various bits of jewellery, was stolen, and his daughter's fiancé Edwin went missing.

## Belt of the Seldarine

The belt of the Seldarine was an artifact dedicated to the elven pantheon, the Seldarine, and a part of a set of artifacts that powered each other as well as the wearer.

Powers
The belt of the Seldarine was a belt enchanted to be a proud and visible symbol of devotion to the Seldarine and the love of the elven deities of their mortal worshipers. The belt of the Seldarine and its more powerful version, the greater belt of the Seldarine, empowered its wearer, increasing their dexterity and charisma. Additionally, it boosted their physical power and skills of deflecting incoming blows and granted a slight ability to siphon their opponents' vitality. When such a belt was worn in tandem with a cloak of the Seldarine and the emblem of the Seldarine, the set also increased the wearer's health and toughness and healed a small part of each incoming blow.

History
The belt of the Seldarine was found in the hands of adventurers and defenders of Neverwinter in the late 15 century DR, during the Elemental Evil crisis and the rise of Tiamat through the Cult of the Dragon's machinations all around northwest Faerûn.

The belt or girdle would imbue the wearer with the strength, though neither size nor girth, of a stone giant. Furthermore some belts and girdles also allowed the wearer to throw rocks and bend metal bars as if they had imbibed a potion of giant strength of the stone giant variety, such that one could throw a  rock for .

 Thunderstorm, the nomad mercenary.

## Beastman

Beastmen were humanoids creatures roughly similarly to short and slender humans, but covered in a green-coloured fur.

Description
Ordinary beastmen were on the smaller side, around  in height. They had two layers of fur, an outer layer of green and an inner of black, as they move the inner black layer instinctively raises and sags, creating patterns in their fur which gives them a natural camouflage in foliage.

Skillful hunters, the beastmen were well adapted to survival in their forests, themselves only hunted by the most cunning and powerful creatures. Although omnivores, they themselves practiced no form of agriculture.

Personality
Due to their natural camouflaging fur they seldom wore clothing or ornamentation of any sort. Their native language was quite intricate, and involved spoken elements, facial expressions, gestures, and changes in their fur-patterns. Thus while non-beastmen could learn the words, expressions, and gestures, they would still be unable to truly communicate without somehow reproducing the color changes. For the beastmen this also means that all other languages they may learn seem simple and even primitive, and gives them a sense of superiority above other speakers.

Most beastmen did not believe in things they could not see, hear, or touch. Some sages claimed that this was due to the beastmen's innate magical resistance.

Combat
Their natural ability to camouflage themselves were a prime focus in most combats involving beastmen. They would attempt to remain undetected, blending in to the foliage and then ambush their enemies. Their natural magic resistance made them dangerous opponents for those who relied on it to make war. The beastmen themselves employed a variety of spears, stone axes, knives, bolas, and blowguns, creating a special toxin they smeared on their weapons and shot from their blowguns. This poison could kill those hit in very short time, but were somewhat easier to resist than many other poisons.

Sometimes they would take prisoners rather than outright kill their foes, and during these times they would drop large weighted nets woven from vines and creepers down on their opponents. Those captured would be stripped of all possessions and then released far from the tribe, if however the beastmen thought those captured would continued to be a threat, they would instead put them to a painless death.

Society
The beastmen tribes usually consisted of 40 to 60 members, and would avoid most contact with outsiders. Each tribe was led by a chieftain who, instead of being elected, appointed or inherited, would simply adopt the position as needed. This meant that the chieftain would be a different beastman most days, and change as the situation warranted. If the tribe was a war, the best warrior would be chief, and if multiple individuals were suited, a competition would take place to decide the days leader. This system only worked due to the fact that it was not considered an honor to be the chief, instead being a duty that would be placed upon one's shoulders from time to time. Likewise there were no shame in never being chief, or losing a competition for the leadership spot.

The beastmen tribes did not discriminate against either sex, except refusing pregnant woman from participating in strenuous activities, like combat. The young was raised communally, and at any one time around 1-in-10 beastmen would be children.

Beastmen lived in tree-houses woven from the living branches of the forest canopy, with 3-6 adult individuals to a house. These groups was a single marriage group, and usually there would be one or two children along with the adults in such a group.

Beastmen tribes had little that other cultures considered worth trading for, their culture was self-sustaining and they had no need for outside trade.

History
In  in Daggerdale, an old dwarven outpost and watchtower called The Eagle's Eyrie, was inhabited by a pair of beastmen and their hunting eagles.

Appendix
See Also
The Cloven Ogre taverns sign were of a beastman having his face cloven in two.

## Belathin

Belathin was a human Red Wizard who moved to Suzail in order to remove himself from the cutthroat culture in Thay and amass power in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Personality
An intelligent manipulative person, Belathin used clever words and powerful charm spells to manipulate his group of followers. He grew up in the Thayan power politics of the Red Wizards, but realized that in order to amass power for himself, he needed to get away from that place, thus his absconding to Suzail.

Possessions
He wore a set of bracers of defense, and a ring of protection, defending himself with a wand of petrification.

## Belt of swimming

The Belt of swimming was a unique magic belt that allowed the wearer to swim very fast and keep warm.

The wearer could swim as fast as a merfolk on the surface, and as a triton underneath, while keeping warm as per a ring of warmth, such that one could swim in even frigid waters. Once the wearer exited the waters the belt would instantly dry them.

## Beckside Castle

Beckside Castle was a fortification located along the High Road in the kingdom of Cormyr, between Tyrluk and Eveningstar. After being destroyed, it was used as a camping spot for travelers.

Structure

The castle was set on a Starwater tributary, with a stone bridge crossing it to the gated entrance. Within the walls were many chambers, including a wine cellar, library, dining hall, and throne room, and a tall remaining tower that served as a beacon for travelers. By the late 15 century DR much of the castle was collapsed and inaccessible, and lower levels flooded, though ways through it did exist.

History

The castle had lain in ruins since the end of the Goblin War in the .

By 1485 DR, travelers and adventurers used the castle ruins as a sheltered campsite when traveling down the High Road, with Breagar Coppervein setting up a small shop to service them. Shortly after the Shadovar invasion of Wheloon, the Weathered Dawn group set up in the ruins, with Tanya Brock and Vicas Oakleaf taking the lead on managing and civilizing the place. Unbeknownst to them, Oriphaun Huntsilver had hidden a scrap of the Abyssal Planus Mechanus Nether Scroll in the ruins as he was pursued by the forces of Thultanthar. The Shadovars Keldan Shadeskein and Gaston Wyrst infiltrated the ruins with a team of mercenaries, bringing shadows and umbral creatures with them and corrupting a local water elemental. They were shortly followed by Freda Gambosi's Harper agents, who spoke with the campers and found their way into the secret areas of the ruins by activating a hidden passage in an old fireplace. The two groups fought in the dilapidated halls of the castle, with the Harpers eventually coming out on top and taking the Nether Scroll. They might also have found the old war relics forgotten light and the roadwatch bow there.

## Belt of bold brilliance

The belt of bold brilliance was a belt found in Myth Drannor during the late 14 century DR.

Powers
The belt boosted the wearer's wits and intelligence, as well as their quick reflexes. It gave wide-ranging assistance to most things, especially healing magics.

History
The belt was originally of dwarf make, and may have been delivered to Myth Drannor as a gift. Whatever the origin, it ultimately ended up in the ruined House of Gems. It may have been found by the Elves of Cormanthor during the Elven Crusade in 1374 DR when they searched for the phylactery of the Red Wizard Droic Orgul there.

## Belub-Zi

Belub-Zi was an influential merchant and the head of his merchant family in the city of Westgate in the late 14 century DR.

History
In the, the homeless beggar named Slicker was collecting coin on the Market Triangle of Westgate, when a nearby building exploded into a pyre. Unlike the destroyed building, a stack of crates owned by Belub-Zi, a well-connected merchant who manned his stall on the Triangle, was safe, as only a madman would dare to explode Belub-Zi's goods. Slicker decided to stick close to them as that was, in his opinion, the safest place in the area.

## Beyard Freemantle

Beyard "Bey" Freemantle was a human warrior and one of the founding members of the Knights of Myth Drannor, originally known as the Swords of Eveningstar, in the mid–14 century DR.

Description
Bey was a muscled warrior with the bearing of a skilled swordsman, appearing alongside his friend Agannor Wildsilver when they first encountered the original Swords.

Personality
Bey was known to snore "like the call of a distant and melancholy war horn" while sleeping.

Relationships
Bey was good friends with Agannor Wildsilver, another warrior who joined the Swords at the same time.

History
In the earliest days of the Knights of Myth Drannor in the, he and his friend Agannor joined the adventuring group while at the The Moon and Stars tavern in Waymoot.

During his time with the Knights, Bey participated in exploring the Haunted Halls in Eveningstar.

He and Agannor ultimately betrayed the Knights when a Zhentarim mage implanted them with mindworms. Under this influence, they murdered Taeriana and Kestra, the two coinlasses who had unwittingly infected them with the mindworms. They soon after killed their fellow Knight, Martess Ilmra, when she discovered their deeds.

Bey met his end later that day while attempting to reach the Zhentarim mage Whisper through a portal, dying when Whisper's magical guardians—a chuul, ettin, and umber hulk—were turned against him. He died alongside Agannor and a Zhentarim agent who had been leading them.

## Belford Willoughby

Belford Willoughby was the son of Harvard Willoughby, a comedian and the host of the "Laff Riot" competition that was held at the Elfsong Tavern in Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Belford looked a lot like his father. He a human boy with a light skin tone, blue eyes, blonde hair, and he was a snappy dresser, wearing a puffy beret decorated with a large quill feather.

History
In the, Harvard Willoughby hosted a stand-up comedy competition called the "Laff Riot." Together with his young son, Belford, who was Harvard's less-than-enthused announcer, the duo occupied Elfsong's side room with a stage and a piano. Harvard would challenge guests to a stand-up competition of jokes in which the winner would be the one who causes the most cheering and applause. Harvard was the usual winner of the show, as the public seemed to enjoy the man's copious puns. He joked about drow comedian who used Tasha's hideous laughter on the attendees and that drow didn't need torches as they had darkvision. His other jokes included references to his wife who cheated on him with a mage who could cast mage hand all night long and that she ran away from him with a fiend, "fiend-zoning" the man. Harvard discouraged jokes about religion and appreciated good porcine humor.

He had a deep voice.

Personality

Activities

After Heldon was torched by Undarl and his dragon, the bandit Helm Stoneblade came at night to investigate with at least two companions, one of which was Bellard. The latter bandit was skeptical that the town and its people had been set ablaze by a dragon, and he was eager to loot the dead.

## Bethgarl Nreams

Bethgarl Nreams was a cheese trader of Athalantar in the mid-3 century DR.

Personality
He was quite determined to sell his cheese.

Activities
He carried a cart of cheeses to trade in Hastarl, pushing it past a hill every time he went on that road. He had also made it a habit of eating certain snails his wife told him not to.

Possessions
He had a very squeaky cart.

Relationships
His wife was Ammuthe.

History
On the day after the slaying of King Belaur Aumar of Hastarl and the coronation of Helm Stoneblade as its new king, Bethgarl Nreams went to Hastarl by early morning, through a road that placed an old grave-shrine to his left on the way to Hastarl. He was startled by the sight of a ball of blue flame there, in which he saw two forms, a man and a woman engaged in some nondescript act. He decided to drop the cart and run.

Later he returned with his wife, who admonished him for dropping the cheeses. However, upon seeing the ball of blue flame, which was still there, she panicked, and ultimately ran away. He had since grown relaxed, and picked up his cheeses, then winked at the ball, and got startled anew as he noticed it winking back, so he made haste to get to Hastarl, where he was surprised by the commotion at the gates.

## Bell of Pavlov

A bell of Pavlov was a type of enchanted bell that when rung caused nearby creatures to salivate uncontrollably.

Description
These bells had the appearance of small, silver handbells.

Powers
When rung a bell of Pavlov affected creatures within a  radius, minus the bell ringer themselves. The rate at which the affected salivated impeded their capacity to talk, consequently making it impossible to cast spells that required verbal components.

Additionally, there was a one-in-five chance that creatures affected by a bell of Pavlov would feel compelled to go have a meal, regardless of whatever action they were in the middle of.

Appendix
Background
This item’s name and effect are probably a reference to neurologist and physiologist Ivan Pavlov and his classical conditioning experiments.

## Bean

A bean was the edible seed of a variety of plants known as legumes, which were cultivated across the continents of Toril, including Faerûn, Kara-Tur, Maztica, and Zakhara. They could be prepared for consumption in a variety of different ways, including baking, stewing, mashed into a paste, or eaten raw as sprouts.

Availability
Areas of the Realms where non-distinct varieties beans could be found cultivated included the following:
 Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold a variety of different beans in its "Aurora's Larder" section, including broad, green, and long beans.
 In the old Calim Empire, the fertile farmlands of the city-state of Cursrah grew beans, including broad and chickpeas.
 In Cormyr, beans were among the staple crops of the small farming town of Eveningstar.
 In the Dalelands, beans were among the crops of farmers in Shadowdale.
 In east Faerûn, despite its lands arid lands typically being poorly suited for farming, beans were among the crops that some farmers in Threskel managed to cultivate.
 In the waters of the Moonsea, there was one small unnamed island whose inhabitants subsisted on part from growing beans.
 In the Shining South, one could find large vats of white beans for sale at some retailers in Vaelan and some private gardens in Halarahh grew their own pole-beans.It is not actually clear what is being grown on a trellis on this page. This article assumes it be beans due to the term "bean trellis" being used, but the description "fat crimson pods" could apply to a number of other produce, such as tamarinds.
 In the nation of Sembia, some of the outer farms of Selgaunt grew beans.
 In the Vast, the villages of Fallentree and Stanham were known to grow beans among other crops. They could also be found for sale in the Ravens Bluff Market Place.
 In the Vilhon Reach, beans could be found cultivated in the nation of Turmish.

Beyond Faerun
 A variety of different beans grew on Kara-Tur's isolated Malatran Plateau.
 In the Hordelands, the isolated kingdom of Delmyr counted beans among its crops.
 On the continent of Maztica, beans were grown alongside other crops in marshland and shallow lakes upon floating gardens. The city of Maxal has such fields of beans.
 In Maztica's Pasocada Basin, beans were one of the staple food crops of the indigenous Azuposi, who typically stored them in ceramic pots.

Varieties
Some beans in the Realms were referred to broadly by the term "pole-bean", as they grew on leafy vines that twisted and climbed up surfaces as they grew. Such beans usually needed some kind of structure for support, known as a bean trellis or beanpole, a common form being that of a tripod of wooden spars.
True Beans
Bag of beans Though ordinary in appearance, the dried beans found in this magic item exploded if dropped, but if properly planted into the ground could produce a wide array of magical effects.
Black bean A variety of bean that was grown in many parts of Kara-Tur.
Broad bean
Cannelloni bean A white bean endemic to the Malatran Plateau that was related to kidney beans.
Chickpea
Euphorbia A flowering desert plant whose beans could be turned into an oil.
Green bean Also known as snap beans or string beans, were a variety of beans distinguished by being harvested and consumed with their enclosing seed pods.
Kidney bean
Lima bean
Long beans A variety of legume endemic to Maztica.
Marrado beans Beans (presumably of the marrado plant) that resembled kidney beans, except blood-red in color. Endemic to eastern Amn, Tethyr, and Valashar. They had a peppery taste and were typically dried for export.
Red bean A variety of bean that was grown in many parts of Kara-Tur.
Runner-vine A pole-bean type of legume found in Cormyr.
Soybean A legume that was a staple crop in many parts of the continent of Kara-Tur.
Umbellin A spicy, brown bean endemic to the Elemental Plane of Fire. it was cultivated by the City of Brass in the nearby Obsidian Fields.

Fake Beans
A number of plants unrelated to legumes commonly had the term "bean" applied to their seeds. These included the following:
Castor bean The seed of a dangerous plant. It was commonly used in wizard laboratories.
Cocoa The small brown seed of a plant endemic to Maztica.
Kaeth Otherwise known as coffee, a large, soft, reddish brown seed that was used to brew an energizing beverage of the same name.
Vanilla A delicate cooking spice and seed of a plant endemic to Maztica.

Culture
 Beans were such versatile ingredients that one could find some cookbooks dedicated to the ways of cooking with them, such as 1001 Ways to Cook Beans and Cobblers and Pies: Baked Beans Can Be Fun.
 The creation myths of the Azuposi told that when they ascended from the realm Shipapu into the True World, the god Masauwu gave them seeds for beans, mayz, and squash, all of which he taught them how to cultivate.
 In the land of T'u Lung, beans were believed to magically correlate with feathered creatures, particularly fowl, as well as the direction of South, the summer season, and elemental fire.

Usages
 Beans were part of a variety of dishes across the Realms, including Lomatran baked beans, Maztican bean stew, potato scones, roasted cockatrice, tortillas, and Zucchini Saiyama style.
 Beans were also a common element of inn pottage across the Realms.
 Dried beans were sometimes placed within sealed pouches, creating a toy known as a bean-bag.
Magic
 Some spells in the Realms could use beans as material components. Gust of wind required the seed of any legume as a material component, whilst Darsson's cooling breeze required one that was dried. The divine version of control vapor required specifically a bean.
 Creating a potion of clarity required brewing the concoction overtop scorched, pungent beans.
 Wizards sometimes made use of legume seeds, such as beans, in a variety of capacities in their laboratories.

Notable Users
Businesses
 The Crock and the Helm, an inn in Skullport.
 The Inn of the Dripping Dagger, an inn and tavern in Waterdeep, offered its patrons mild or hotly spiced bowls of cooked beans for 3 cp.
 Old Troll's Foot, a restaurant in Kirinwood, offered its patrons a spicy broth of artichokes, asparagus, and beans.
Individuals
 Hephtos, a Vergadainan priest and street vendor in Ravens Bluff who generously handed out bean & grain based dishes in the city's Market Place. He was such a fan of the vegetables that he had authored both 1001 Ways to Cook Beans and Cobblers and Pies: Baked Beans Can Be Fun.
 Storm Silverhand grew pole-beans in her home's kitchen garden.

Organizations
 The Everlasting, the largest holy slayer organization in Zakhara, often kept their fortress Sarahin's kitchen granary stocked with beans.
Races & Sentient Creatures
 Many cloud giants on Toril were avid gardeners, growing gigantic versions of produce, such as beans the size of turnips.
 It was not uncommon to find fields of beans in halfling villages.

History
Sometime in the 14 century DR, as part of a stage performance at the Ale & Hearty in the city of Schamedar, the wizard Wiglaf used his magical abilities to conjure a great sky-reaching crop of vegetables that included beans.

In the, three Chosen of Silvanus got together on Sapra with the leaders of the Turmishan druidic order known as the Emerald Enclave and conducted a magical ritual that ended a famine that had been plaguing that nation. This caused all manner of food to instantaneously grow throughout the land, including beans.

## Begging fakery

Begging fakery was a scam recognized across the Realms. The beggar would acquire crutches, bandages and other assorted medical tools to fake injury and pull at people's heartstrings for coin.

Aurora despised the practice and her Emporium refused to sell items for such purposes, but she grudgingly admitted that an interested scammer could create this image with individually bought items from the Emporium's medical section.

## Berronar's cap

Berronar's cap was a type of mushroom found in Faerûn and likely named for Berronar Truesilver. It was used to make healing draughts and salves when combined with vaundyr vine.

History
When the forces of Neverwinter attempted to secure the Blackdagger Ruins, Alinda Greenwood sought out Berronar's caps and vaundyr vines to make medicines with, in order to treat their many injuries. The mushrooms grew widely in the abandoned mines around the region.

## Belarian

Belarian was a well-off elvish businessman and inhabitant of the City Core of Neverwinter in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Belarian was a proud and fair-skinned elven man with a full head of long, golden hair.

Relationship
Belarian held the Head Gaoler Captain Alaefin of the Peninsula District in high regard. He considered the inhabitants of the Beggar's Nest to be nothing but filth.

Activities
During the Wailing Death plague of the, Belarian could be found strolling the streets of the City Core, safe away from the dangers of the quarantined and zombie-infested Beggar's Nest and the convict-overrun Peninsula District. He was ready to offer advice and opinion on the city's districts to the Hero of Neverwinter. He also had some insight about the cure being assembled by Khelben Arunsun and disrupted by a recent attack on Neverwinter Academy, according to Belarian's trusted associate.

## Bethany

Bethany was a well-off human inhabitant of Neverwinter's Peninsula District in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Bethany was an elegant human woman with a light skin tone, brown eyes, and brown hair. She wore fanciful clothing and jewelry.

Relationship
Bethany did not know Head Gaoler Captain Alaefin of the Peninsula District personally, but she believed him to have always been an efficient warden.

History
During the time the Wailing Death ravaged Neverwinter in the, Bethany's home district, the Peninsula, fell to rioting escaped prisoners of the Neverwinter prison. She escaped the district and headed to the City Core to seek help from members of the Neverwinter militia. Sedos Sebile, one of the trusted agents of Nasher Alagondar, was in the rioting district, according to Bethany, but did not have nearly enough people under her command.

## Bensen Virandson

Bensen Virandson was a wizard and Harper agent who worked in Cormyr in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Bensen had short white hair and muttonchops.

Personality

Bensen was somewhat sardonic and jaded, and hard to impress or surprise.

Abilities
Bensen was a proficient and talented mage, and in particular knew an array of defensive spells that could keep him safe, at the cost of limiting him solely to a defensive posture.

History

Following the Shadovar invasion of Wheloon in the mid-1480s DR, Rina Belostos and her Harpers searched for Oriphaun Huntsilver and the Abyssal Planus Mechanus Nether Scroll that he had taken from the Shadovar at Wheloon Prison. Knowing that the forces of Thultanthar were on his tail, Oriphaun tore the Scroll to pieces and hid them along the High Road to confuse the pursuit and give the Harpers time to catch up. Bensen managed to quickly locate a piece in the eaves of the King's Forest, but his inside source reported on the appearance of Shadovar forces between him and the Harper Lodge where it could be safely contained. He waited for Harper reinforcements at the Greenweald Lane gatehouse, and was displeased when he merely received trustworthy adventurers sent by Rina.

With nothing else to wait for, Bensen carried the Scroll piece, protecting it and himself with powerful magics while his escort fought their way through roadblocks and ambushes of Netherese wizards, hired sellswords, and Dom's team of elite mercenaries. Along the way, the tumult of combat caused the Scroll to randomly summon dretches from the Abyss, adding extra chaos to the series of running battles. When the dust finally settled, he was able to store and hide the artifact piece at the Harper Lodge until it could be safely moved on.

## Ben Garmond

Ben Garmond was a trader from Procampur in the Vast in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
He was huge, and dressed in fine clothes.

Activities
His route took him once a year to meet with Stag Falls traders in Eaglton, where he got quartz and furs he sold at Ravens Bluff and Tantras for a nifty profit.

Relationships
He and his brother Garrold worked together.

History
After Romanisk Vlaensk, a vampire wizard, was released at Stag Falls and took control of the village one year around the, the traders of Stag Falls could no longer go to Eaglton. When Ben and his brother Garrold reached Eaglton, they found none to do business with, and rather than go to Stag Falls, they preferred to wait there. Their patience ran out after two weeks, on the day when investigators from Ravens Bluff, who'd been hired by Marcus Rubicon, arrived whilst headed for Stag Falls. If questioned, the brothers may have said they believed there had been no traders coming because of the muddy ground.

## Beomult Ragner

Beomult Ragner was the judge of Eaglton and innkeeper at the Eagle's Eyrie inn of that same town in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Activities
Though mostly an innkeeper at the Eagle's Eyrie, of which he was the owner, he acted as a judge in town, with the authority to call for imprisonments and impose fines.

History
During the mid-to-late 14 century DR, during the spring when the vampire wizard Romanisk Vlaensk had been released from under Stag Falls, Beomult Ragner was an innkeep at the Eagle's Eyrie in the nearby town of Eaglton, where he appeared to have been working for at least one season, remembering when traders from Stag Falls usually arrived.

During one evening when agents of Ravens Bluff hired by Marcus Rubicon passed by, he may have shared some information with them regarding the path to Stag Falls, the harsh winter they'd last had, and the patron from Ravens Bluff that had went towards Stag Falls the week prior. He'd also noticed that no one had come from Stag Falls that spring. Through the course of the evening, he might also have had the opportunity to put the thief Louie in lockup, depending on how his attempts at pickpocketing these men went.

He might also fine them the next morning if for whatever reason they damaged the apiary.

## Beau

Beau was the pseudodragon familiar bonded to Methrammar Aerasumé, a wizard and fighter who served as the Argent Legion's High Marshal, by the late 14 century DR. The two were inseparable, with Beau often accompanying his master into danger. Luckily, the tiny dragon-kin was typically protected by Methrammar's spells and a magic item that Beau kept in his possession.

Personality
Beau's unpredictable actions were somewhat at odds with Methrammar's affection for the creature, with his behavior sometimes baffling even his master.

Abilities
Natural
As both a pseudodragon and a familiar, Beau had traits typical of such creatures. These included, but were not limited to, the ability to speak telepathically, so long as Beau's target spoke either Sylvan or Common, to perpetually see invisibility, and even to maintain an empathic link between him and Methrammar if the two were within  of one another. Through his stinger, Beau was also capable of delivering a sleep-inducing poison.

External
Beyond his own, natural defenses, Beau was often protected even further by Methrammar's powerful magic. The specific spell chosen, however, could vary. Beau's master sometimes went as far as casting a magic circle against evil or kept it as simple as a basic protection from arrows.

Possessions
Beau counted on his ring of protection +2 as his sole piece of equipment, which helped keep him safe if ever he found himself in terrible danger.

Relationships
Being Methrammar's familiar, Beau became a kind of constant companion to the half-elf, remaining at his side frequently—even with danger present. Methrammar showed a deep affection for Beau, protecting him with powerful magic and keeping him safe.

## Bey

Bey  was a title used for positions of authority across several different cultures.

Genie Titles
Among the genie races, bey was one of many common titles held by noble djinn and noble efreet, who served the Great Caliph of the Djinn or Sultan of the Efreet, respectively.

When at court, a bey might be addressed by additional titles. Among djinn this was "Lord of the Four Quarters", while for efreet it was "Lord of Flames". The especially favored among the bey were granted the privilege of appending a particular color to that designation, with darker colors denoting greater prestige. The highest of these one could achieve was "Lord of the Black Flames".

Beys were a rank below emirs in efreet society. The best of beys, a "Lord of the Black Flames", would be promoted to the station of emir if they did well on a field of battle. Both stations were responsible for a thurgur, or military area, distant colonies across the vast Elemental Plane of Fire that could contain one to four fortresses. The efreet beys viewed this services to the Sultan as a necessary burden for the good of their whole race. However, they kept their thurgur strictly functional and spartanly military, affording the people under their command few comforts.

Taangan Titles
In the Hordelands, prior to the 15 century DR, the title "bey" could be found in use in the nation of Murghôm. This went as far back as the days of ancient Netheril, when mortal Myrkul was known as Myrkul Bey al-Kursi.

The title was a singular position within the nation, known fully as the "Bey of Murghôm". Those who held the title had the power to appoint emirs to rule over a location the bey specified. Furthermore, the bey could rule and enact laws in a city, such as Zindalankh, offering protection in return. To attack a city that was under the Bey's protection would be considered a declaration of war against Murghôm as a whole.

Trivia
 During the Shadowed Age of Netheril, there was a powerful warrior from the Low Netheril port city of Runlatha known as the Bey of Runlatha. Over time his name would become bastardized in the Illuskan language, compounding into Beorunna and later Berun.Among that sources that discuss Beorunna, it is not explained why he had the moniker of "Bey", but he was definitely not the city's ruler.

## Beccard Rellock

Beccard Rellock was a caravaneer somewhere in the Savage Frontier.

Personality
Beccard was often shortsighted despite his scheming mind. He loved his son.

Activities
Beccard spent most of his time on the road, but returned once a year to a remote logging camp somewhere between the Sword Coast to the west, and the Anauroch to the east.

Relationships
The Harpers considered Beccard to aspire to their status. He had a young son, Nollo Rellock.

History
A caravaneer most of the year, Beccard Rellock had been stopping at a logging camp somewhere between Anauroch and the Sword Coast for years, where he made some exhibits. In, that exhibit had been a "beholder", which was actually made of a sack of potatoes, while in, he'd exhibited a drow who was actually the daughter of a local chimney sweeper, still black with dirt, per the recollection of a local kid. According to his son, the lack of interest in his exhibits frustrated him to no end.

In, Beccard Rellock somehow acquired a brass dragon wyrmling, which he hid in a barn at his logging camp, planning on selling or exhibiting it somewhere, for money. However, the brass dragon attracted the attention of a larger, older, hostile blue dragon, as well as the wyrmling's mother. The blue dragon attacked the lumber camp where Beccard was; it fled after finding the wyrmling in the barn, where it met with the brass dragon's mother. Beccard was reprimanded by the Harpers for this.

The chase between the dragons extended from that barn to a wooden mill; he found his son Nollo under a collapsed wagon in the barn where the dragons' chase started. Despite the damage, his son was quite chipper about having been able to exhibit the dragon to his friends for three copper pieces, one of which was for touching the dragon.

Reputation
One of the local kids thought of Beccard as a liar whose exhibits were underwhelming and invariably fake.

## Beholder worship

Beholder worship was the practice of non-beholders venerating beholders or their deities like the Great Mother, usually under the direct supervision of a tyrannical beholder overlord.

Description

Many beholders, particularly the sane ones, found that charmed minions could worship the beholder as a god. Even better, larger and larger organizations of minions could be operated indirectly by using charmed or indoctrinated servants to direct them. This cult worship provided the beholder with a delightful sense of satisfaction and self-importance (even from such lesser creatures), in addition to the more material purposes that the organization could be bent to such as a thieves' guild or political apparatus. Larger confluences of beholders working together—clusters, hives, and hive cities—continued this pattern, using increasing numbers of slaves and servants to provide material and worshipful value.

There were a number of beholder cults across Faerûn, particularly among barbarian tribes and city dwellers who felt oppressed or slighted by their rulers. In many cases the focus of these cults were fictional beholder deities such as "Aurauth Manyeyes", "Xangolgaerath", and "Yaerethontur". The humanoid cultists believed that they served a great beholder god and the present beholders were demigods or divine servants of it, but this was simply a fiction created to ensure loyal and zealous followers.

Ocular adepts were the rare individuals who entered the service of a beholder and took a gazer's eyeball as an implanted organ. They then served as the middlemen for a beholder, managing their servants and leading the worship of the Great Mother. They were actually fully-fledged clerics of the Great Mother, though they received lesser powers due to being non-beholders. Other "high priests" of beholders could instead gain abilities from a kind of shamanic tradition.

History

The beholders that formed the Alimir Hive plagued the Lake of Steam region with repeated wars and schemes for thousands of years, aiming to enslave the local human populations. From  to  they conquered and ruled the Calimshan Empire before being driven out by the founders of the Drakhon Dynasty. Then in the years leading up to the Eye Tyrant Wars in, many beholders established cults within the sewers of cities across the Lands of Intrigue, planning to one day have them pour out and conquer the cities when they had grown strong enough.

Another spate of conquest and cults occurred in the Lake of Steam region between 409 DR and 430 DR during the Eye Battles. Later, the Ring of Eyes set themselves up with cults in Cortryn from 731 DR, before being destroyed by Tashara of the Seven Skulls in 757 DR.

The beholder known only as the "Unseeing Eye" formed the monotheistic Cult of the Eyeless in the city of Berdusk, then was caught and thought destroyed by Thom Wainwright. It later re-emerged in 1369 DR in the sewers of Athkatla to reform its cult, only to be slain by Abdel Adrian.

The Daeth Haeromm was an alliance of beholders which utilized a "High Priest of the Tyrants" in the 15 century DR, ostensibly serving the "Dread One", a fictional deity that even the High Priest Ulchaice Darrovyn knew was false.

The infamous "City of Cults" Kormul attracted many strange followings in its time, including the Risen Eye beholder cult.

## Berserker's elemental chainmail

Berserker's elemental chainmail was a masterwork scalemail shirt created by some of the most experienced mailsmiths of Neverwinter, the City of Skilled Hands, in the late 15 century DR.

Description
A berserker's elemental chainmail was created from a high-quality, ornate scale shirt crafted of mithral rings and scales by Neverwintran mailsmiths. Once an ornate scale shirt was crafted, it was enchanted using elemental aggregates. A stronger variant of this magic armor, berserker's exquisite elemental chainmail, was further enchanted using a dragon egg gem and unified elements. There existed two variants of this tier of enchantment – berserker's high-quality exquisite elemental chainmail, and berserker's gemmed exquisite elemental chainmail. The most powerful variant was berserker's superior elemental chainmail, created from an exquisite set of chainmails with further use of unified elements and an arcane shard. Similarly, there were two other variants – berserker's high-quality elemental chainmail and berserker's gemmed elemental chainmail..

Powers
All versions of this set of magic chainmail offered the wearer various levels of enhanced physical power, defense, and increased their ability to land devastating and well-aimed strikes. Exquisite and superior sets also boosted the wearer's ability to penetrate enemy armor with their attacks.

History
These masterworks of the mailsmithing art were known to be created by some of the best craftsmen of Neverwinter in the late 15 century DR.
